# Original verification ledger: Interface windows and audio

Part of the [original verification ledger](../original-verification-ledger.md) (ARC-RES-048): its [inputs and method](../original-verification-ledger.md#inputs-and-method), [verdicts](../original-verification-ledger.md#verdicts-and-what-they-mean-for-the-two-settings), [decisions to revisit](../original-verification-ledger.md#decisions-to-revisit) and [second reading](../original-verification-ledger.md#second-reading) cover every section below. A new section of this area goes at the end of this file and adds one line to the ledger's [area index](../original-verification-ledger.md#areas).

## Audio (ARC-RES-052)

Source: the [audio study](../audio-evidence.md#what-the-original-does), rows SN1–SN34, which keeps the
full table (SN34: one reading, ARC-WORLD-041). Three domains (resources and positional sound, schemes and combat music, voice and
event sounds) were each read by a first reader and, independently, a second reader that saw only
the questions (method step 6); the three differences are settled in the study. Every direct
branch of the functions read stays inside `.text`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SN3 | `gsound_init` (0x41AA30) | soundparams key 12 is the small size's volume (default 40), key 22 the medium's (70); no overwrite. | Reconstruction slip |
| SN4 | `gsound_calc_positional_params` (0x41B420) | The attenuated volume is multiplied by the size's volume % (small 40, medium 70, large 100). | Reconstruction slip |
| SN12 | `gsound_scheme_load` (0x41BE20), `gsound_scheme_parse_entry` (0x41BF70) | A 26th entry in one scheme dereferences a null pointer (crash). | Original bug (no installed scheme reaches it; both settings refuse) |
| SN13 | `gsound_scheme_parse_entry` (0x41BF70) | `/over` wins over `/loop`; a balance-left above 127 clamps to 127. | Reconstruction slip |
| SN14 | `gsound_update` (0x41BAF0) | At most one ambient per slot per tick; hour windows never wrap, so 20 installed night entries never play. | Reconstruction slip (one per tick); original bug (windows), rule switch (ARC-UI-041) |
| SN17 | `gsound_start_combat_music` (0x41C4D0) | An NPC entering combat starts stinger and loop together; the local PC leaving combat restores the latest request. | Original |
| SN18 | `gsound_save` (0x41B1D0), `gsound_load` (0x41B0F0) | A save made during combat loses the pre-combat schemes. | Original bug, rule switch (ARC-WORLD-040) |
| SN22 | `dialog_ui_speech_start` (0x5688D0) | Voice gender is the PC's; closing the dialog does not stop the voice. | Original |
| SN24 | `sfx_critter_sound` (0x4F0ED0) | The off flag is tested on the local PC, not the sounding critter. | Reconstruction slip |
| SN26 | `AGbeginAnimLoopAnim` (0x42DA50), eye candy (0x431550, 0x431960) | Looping scenery and eye-candy sounds loop; flag bit 0x80; handle test and goal fields as written in the study. | Reconstruction slip |
| SN31 | hot-key equip (0x550000) | Equipping into an empty slot plays the slot number as a sound id. | Original bug, switch candidate |

## Logbook (ARC-UI-008)

Source: the [viewer logbook](../../rendering/terrain-viewer.md#logbook-arc-ui-008), the [story domain](../../world/game-session-runtime.md#story-domain)
and [FR-SOCIAL-008](../../requirements/story-social-ai.md). Question: what the original logbook shows on its rumors and quests
pages, which text it reads, how it opens and how a rumor is quelled. Read on 2026-10-18 by a first reader and,
independently, a second reader that saw only the questions (method step 6); both agree on every row. Every direct branch
stays inside `.text`; the indirect ones (the key handler's character table, the tab switch of the list builder, the dialog
action table) stay inside their functions. LB8 is a slip of the reconstruction (the first verdict of the table above), so
no rule switch; Steamwoven reads both files as the original does.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LB1 | in-game message filter (0x54B5D0) | The character messages `l` and `L` (one case of the filter's character table, next to `c`/`C` of CS1) call the logbook toggle with the local PC, under the guards of CS1 (not while another critter acts in turn-based combat, a text edit owns the keys or the main menu is active); an interface sound follows every handled letter. The interface's logbook button calls the same toggle. A keyboard key for the logbook was not found in the earlier reading of the key handlers; the executable has one. | Original |
| LB2 | logbook toggle (0x53F020), logbook close (0x53F090) | Already open: closes it (a toggle). Otherwise a null object returns; a dead **local PC** (not the argument) refuses; the interface mode is set to main, then to the logbook, either refusal refuses; then the object is stored and the window created. Close sets the main mode, then destroys the window and forgets the object. | Original |
| LB3 | logbook create (0x53F0E0), destroy (0x53F2F0), panel draw (0x53F490), tab switch (0x53F5F0), reset (0x53EFD0) | The tab and the page spread are kept across close and open: opening rebuilds the lists and redraws the kept spread. Clicking a tab resets the page to the first spread; a game reset (load, new game) sets the rumors tab and the first page. Holding left Ctrl and left Alt while opening shows a hidden page of developer quotes in place of the rumors. Neither opening nor creating pauses the game. | Original |
| LB4 | entry timestamp (0x5403C0), rumor entry (0x540310), quest entry (0x540470) | An entry starts with a date line: the month name (message month + 6 of `mes\logbk_ui.mes`), the day, a comma, the year, two spaces, the hour on a 12-hour clock (hour 0 is 12 am, hour 12 is 12 pm) with two minute digits and "am"/"pm", then four spaces. A quest entry appends its state label (message state + 19) right after it. A newline follows, then the rumor's text or the quest's description. | Original |
| LB5 | logbook list builder (0x53FBB0), rumors and quests tabs | Rumors: struck through in black when quelled, plain black otherwise. Quests: accepted and achieved in blue; completed struck through in green; completed by someone else and botched struck through in red; mentioned in plain black. | Original |
| LB6 | quest logbook list (0x4C52E0), its comparator (0x4C53A0) | Only a PC has quests. Every quest record whose state without the botched bit is not Unknown is listed (a quest botched while still Unknown is not), reported Botched when the botched bit is set and with its stored state otherwise, then sorted by the date-time of its last change, oldest first, with the run-time library's sort, which is not stable: the order of equal times is the sort's. | Original |
| LB7 | quest description (0x4C5250) | The output starts empty; the low-intelligence description is used when one is loaded and the object's intelligence is 4 or less, otherwise the normal one; with neither the description stays empty. | Original |
| LB8 | quest module load (0x4C4670) | `rules\gamequest.mes` is parsed for quests 1000..1999; only when that file loads are `mes\gamequestlog.mes` (normal descriptions) and `mes\gamequestlogdumb.mes` (low-intelligence descriptions) loaded, each searched for 1000..1999 in its own file; the second is tried even when the first is missing. The earlier reading searched the normal file for both, which made the low-intelligence descriptions the normal ones. | Reconstruction slip |
| LB9 | rumor logbook list (0x4C5A40), its comparator (0x4C5AF0) | Every rumor 1000..2999 with a non-zero learning time is listed with its quelled mark, sorted by that time, oldest first, with the same unstable sort. | Original |
| LB10 | rumor logbook text (0x4C5920, 0x4C5960, 0x4C59D0), rumor module load (0x4C5570) | Module load reads `mes\game_rd_npc_m2m.mes`, `…_m2f`, `…_f2f`, `…_f2m` and `…_m2m_dumb`. A rumor's logbook text is message 20 × number of the first file, or of the last one when the object's intelligence is 4 or less; a missing message gives an empty text, with no fallback to the other file. | Original |
| LB11 | rumor quell (0x4C5690), multiplayer lock test (0x4A2BA0) | In single player the rumor's bit (number − 1000) of a game-wide bitmap is set; there is no range check. A multiplayer game sends a message as well. No function clears a bit except the module load. | Original |
| LB12 | dialog action executor (0x415BA0) | `RQ n` quells rumor *n* with no participant and no PC test; `RU n` marks rumor *n* known to the dialog's PC. | Original |
| LB13 | date-time hour (0x45A870), minute (0x45A8B0), day (0x45A8E0), month (0x45A900), year (0x45A920) | From the (days, milliseconds) pair, unsigned: hour = ms / 3,600,000 mod 24, minute = ms / 60,000 mod 60, day = days mod 30 + 1, month = days / 30 mod 12 + 1, year = start year + days / 360. | Original |
| LB14 | window table 0x5C33F8 (16-byte rectangles), create 0x53F0E0, destroy 0x53F2F0, window callback 0x53F350, panel draw 0x53F490, tab switch 0x53F5F0 (ARC-UI-074) | The window is the game windows' 800 × 400 at the screen's (0, 41). Create blits 264 `LogBooks_Side` at (0, 0), makes the two page buttons (art 272 at (213, 77), 273 at (675, 77)) and seven tab buttons (arts 265, 267, 271, 266, 270, 268, 269 at x 696, the fourth at 695, y 83, 129, 175, 221, 267, 313, 359; one radio group, 0x538670, whose pressed member is the current tab's button; the table's order is the tab index) and registers the PC lens (art 198 over the rectangle (25, 24, 89, 89), the inventory's round-view box 0x550DA0). The panel draw blits 260 `Book_Main` at (165, 0) and 261 at (172, 23), the only page art any tab uses (259 and 262 are not called here). Sounds: open 3008, close 3009, tab switch 3011 then the new tab's page 3010, page turn 3010. The callback has no keyboard branch: a left release (mouse subtype 1; LB23 corrects "press") inside the lens rectangle (0x551000) closes the window through 0x53F090; a tab button's release switches the tab and its hover prints the tab's title message as a hint. | Original |
| LB15 | spread layout 0x53F8F0, entry fill 0x53F9E0 (jump table 0x53FAAC, one builder per tab), entry text draw 0x53FAD0, next page 0x53F640, previous page 0x53F6A0, spread redraw 0x53F6E0 | A spread has two text columns (222, 72, 215, 270) and (468, 72, 215, 270) and shows two pages numbered 2k + 1 and 2k + 2. Entries go in list order from a column's top, each wrapped to the column's width, a gap of one text line (the plain style's one-line height) between two entries and none after the list's last. An entry that does not fit the column's remaining height is not split: the left column retries it whole at the top of the right column; the right column ends the spread and the entry starts the next one. An entry that fits closes its column when its height plus the gap is not less than the remaining height (left: the right column continues; right: the spread ends). Only the first entry placed in a fresh column (the spread's start, or the right column's) may be cut: trailing characters are removed one at a time until its wrapped height fits (the first time logs "Had to shorten logbook entry"), so an entry taller than a column loses its tail. An entry with no text only closes the left column (nothing is drawn) and, in the right column, ends the spread without advancing. The next spread starts at the entry after the last one consumed; at most 100 spread starts are kept (0x64879C). The previous button shows when the spread's start is above 0, the next when the last consumed entry is before the list's end; both are hidden at each redraw. A tab switch (0x53F5F0 with the keep flag clear) resets the starts and page 1; a rebuild of the open book redraws the kept spread. | Original |
| LB16 | text styles built at 0x53EB10 (44-byte records: flags, font art, two colours), list builder 0x53FBB0 | Entry text is font art 229 (`Flare12Font`) in black unless the style says otherwise; the style flag 0x20 draws a line through the text in the second colour, equal to the text's. Rumors: quelled (the rumor's bit, 0x4C5650) black struck, otherwise black plain. Quests: record types 2 and 3 (accepted, achieved) blue (0, 0, 150), 4 (completed) green (0, 120, 0) struck, 5 and 6 (completed by someone else, botched) red (150, 0, 0) struck, other types black plain. | Original |
| LB17 | spread redraw 0x53F6E0, message files loaded at 0x53EB10, date line 0x5403C0 | `mes\logbk_ui.mes` and `mes\quotes.mes` load at the module's start. The title of a spread is the tab index's message of `logbk_ui.mes` (0 for rumors, 1 for quests, up to 6), framed as "-title-" (format 0x5C3588), in font art 27 (`morph15font`), centred in both title rectangles (236, 43, 187, 19) and (482, 43, 187, 19); the page numbers are "-N-" (0x5C3580) in font 229, centred in (222, 346, 215, 15) and (468, 346, 215, 15), both drawn on every spread even when the right column is empty. The date line's month is message 6 + month and a quest's state label message 19 + state of the same file (LB4); a missing line prints "Error! Missing line N in FILE". | Original |
| LB18 | list builder branches 0x53FDB0 (reputations) and 0x540130 (background), PC reputation list 0x4C1C30, reputation adder 0x4C1D20, reputation name 0x4C1BD0, entry builders 0x540510 and 0x540760, background description 0x4C26D0 and text 0x4C2530 (ARC-UI-079) | Reputations: only a PC (type 15) has a list, read in stored order (the numbers of field 0xFF, the times of field 0x100; the adder skips a number already held, so the order is the order of acquisition); no sort, no header, no count. Every entry is plain text: the date line (LB4), a line feed, and the name of the reputation in `mes\gamereplog.mes` (numbers of 1000 or more, no upper bound; a missing message leaves the second line empty, no error text). Background: the PC's description number (object field 0x102, unguarded) and its text (0x4C2530, BD6: description 0 reads 1000, the shown part after the first line feed; 1..999 or a missing message gives no text, and the branch then calls the string length on a null pointer, which faults in the retail executable: an original crash, not reproduced; Steamwoven shows an empty page). The text is measured at the column width (215) in the plain style: at most the column height (270) it is one entry; taller, it is cut into pieces: each piece ends after the last space or line feed whose text before it (the break character not counted) still measures within 270, the scan stopping at the first break that does not; the break character stays in the piece; the rest is measured again until it fits, and a text with no break that fits stays one piece. Each piece is its own plain entry with no date line or title, laid out by LB15 (the gap line follows every entry but the last). | Original (the crash: original bug) |
| LB19 | list builder branch 0x53FE30 (blessings and curses), PC blessing list 0x4C4200 (fields 0x104 ids, 0x105 times), PC curse list 0x4C3D50 (0x106, 0x107), time compare 0x45A970, entry builder 0x540550, name functions 0x4C4150 / 0x4C3CA0 (ARC-UI-080) | Only the player's lists are read (the open function 0x53F020 takes the PC handle; the getters return nothing for an object that is not a PC). The two lists are merged, not sorted: while both have entries the one with the earlier time (0x45A970: days, then milliseconds, unsigned) goes first and **on equal times the blessing goes first**; each list is assumed to be in ascending time order as it was appended. No header, filter, de-duplication or count limit (the stack buffers hold 100 records of each list, unchecked: an original overrun past 100, not reproduced; the session caps a list well below). A blessing is the blue plain style (0, 0, 150), a curse the red plain style (150, 0, 0), neither struck. Every entry is plain: the date line (LB4), a line feed, and the name from message 10 × number of `mes\gamebless.mes` (blessings) or `mes\gamecurse.mes` (curses); a number below 50 or a missing message leaves the name empty (the entry is the date line alone). Empty lists draw nothing and the next-page button stays off. | Original (the overrun: original bug) |
| LB20 | list builder branch 0x5402D0 (keys), key list getter 0x463110, key ring finder 0x4631A0, entry builder 0x5407B0, key name 0x4D4260, key add 0x466A50 with its callers 0x466A00 and 0x466AA0 (ARC-UI-081) | Only the player's keys are listed. The list is the key ring's array of key numbers (object field 0xC2) in stored order: no sort, no de-duplication, no limit (the logbook's buffers hold 3000 numbers, unchecked: an original overrun, not reproduced; the session caps the list at 3000). The ring is the first key-ring item (type 12) of the PC's inventory with a non-empty list, else the last ring seen, else none; loose key items (type 11) are not listed. Every entry is plain black text, one line per key: message *number* of `mes\gamekey.mes` (the key number itself, no offset), no date line, no header. A missing message makes the original copy a null string (a fault: an original crash, not reproduced; Steamwoven shows an empty line). A key is added by appending its number at the end of the ring's array: no duplicate check, so a held number appears again; the key item is destroyed and the ring's art updated. A key item inserted into a critter holding a ring is folded into it this way; a key item inserted with no ring stays an ordinary item (no ring is created); a ring inserted into a critter folds the loose keys (last slot first) or merges another ring's numbers into the held one in reverse order. No keys or no ring lists nothing. The session keeps the numbers only (schema 77), not the key and ring items (NOT READY: the key and ring items; since ARC-WORLD-123 the list feeds the lock check, the match rule 0x463340 being `LockRules.KeyFits`, and a key item the hero takes is folded into it, [KY1–KY5](items.md#keys-on-the-heros-ring-arc-world-123)). | Original (the overruns and the missing-message fault: original bugs) |
| LB21 | list builder branch 0x53FF50 (kills and injuries), PC logbook field 0x10C (kill slots getter 0x4F5590, injury getters 0x4F5770 / 0x4F57C0), fixed-line text 0x5405C0, injury text 0x540550 path, kill writer 0x4F5330 with its only caller, the death function 0x45DA20, injury writer 0x4F5690 with its five callers in the damage function 0x4B4390 (ARC-UI-082) | Only the PC's data is read, all in its logbook field 0x10C (an int array): [0] the kill total; pairs [1,2] highest-level, [3,4] lowest-level, [5,6] most positive alignment, [7,8] most negative alignment, [9,10] highest positive and [11,12] most negative magick/tech aptitude, each a victim description number and the value (stats 0x11, 0x13, 0x10); from index 64 the injuries, pairs of the causer's description number and a kind (0 blinded, 1 crippled arm, 2 crippled legs, 3 scar), the list ending at the first zero number. The tab is nine fixed plain lines, then the injuries oldest first, no date line anywhere: line 0 is message 26 of `mes\logbk_ui.mes` and the kill total; lines 1..6 are messages 27..32 and the victim's name (`mes\description.mes` below 30000, the module's `mes\gamedesc.mes` from 30000, 0x4D4210) or "-----" when the number is not above 0 or has no text, the values themselves are not shown; line 7 is empty (an empty entry ends the left column or the spread, LB15); line 8 is message 33; an injury is message 34 + kind, a space and the causer's name (an empty line when the name has no text). The empty-list tab shows the nine lines with "0" and "-----". Every injury starts black struck through and the PC's critter flags (field 0xDA) un-strike the current ones, searching from the newest line down: flag 0x80 the newest blinding, 0x400 the newest crippled legs, 0x200 the newest crippled arm and then 0x100 the next older one (the search shares one cursor, so a missing 0x200 line leaves 0x100 nothing), and the count of effect 50 on the PC (scars) the newest that many scars. Writers: the kill record (0x4F5330) runs when an NPC dies, its death is not blocked by script trigger 12, its flag 0x1 is clear and the killer is a PC or an NPC led up to a PC; it adds one to the total and replaces a slot when the slot has no name yet or the new value is at least (at most for the lowest) the held one, so a tie goes to the newest; zero alignment and zero aptitude count for neither; the victim's number is its description as the PC knows it (field 0x17 when known, else 0xF0). The injury record (0x4F5690) is written only for a PC victim and a PC or NPC attacker: the damage function adds a scar every time a hit scars (and effect 50), a blinding and a crippled-legs record only when the flag was clear, a crippled-arm record when 0x100 was clear, else on a roll of 1..100 at most 50 when 0x200 was clear; the causer is 30286 for a self-inflicted one, 30287 for another PC, else the attacker's description. Buffers of 3000 entries hold the lines; the builder does not check the count (an overrun past 2991 injuries: original bug, not reproduced; the session keeps at most 2000, and refuses a causer number below 1, which would cut the original's list). The session keeps the log (snapshot schema 78), strike state from the hero's life record (conditions and scar count); the feed from combat is LB22 (ARC-UI-097). | Original (the overrun and the cut list: original bugs; the arm cursor quirk kept) |
| LB22 | injury writer 0x4F5690 (guard, cause, first free pair), kill writer 0x4F5330 (arguments, stats 0x11 / 0x13 / 0x10 through the stat getter 0x4B0490), recognition 0x45F970, the five calls in the damage function 0x4B4390 (ARC-UI-097) | Read on 2026-11-08 in the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 `9bd80511f8663a41a25a20a701959e394e0065e4ffc68a8dc69fe19c23678d52`) by this task (F1, one reader; every branch stays inside `.text`). **Injury writer guard:** it returns when the attacker handle is null, when the victim's type is not 15 (a PC), or when the attacker's type is neither 15 nor 16; the earlier F0 reading of the combat requirement ("exits for a critter, so the self and PC branches prove nothing") was a reconstruction slip, not a bug of the game. The cause: the same handle for victim and attacker → 30286; an attacker of type 15 → 30287; else 0x45F970(attacker, victim): the attacker's description field when the attacker is the victim or its reaction memory holds the victim (slots 1..9, NR1/NR3), else its not-yet-met description field. The record goes to the first free pair from index 64 (no bound check). **Damage function:** the five calls come in a hit's order scar (every time the critical-effect scar bit is set, whatever the scar count, kind 3), blinding (when the flag was clear, kind 0), arm (the first arm, kind 1; else the second at d100 ≤ 50, kind 1), legs (when the flag was clear, kind 2); the attacker handle is the damage context's, null for damage with none. **Kill writer** (arguments: the PC, then the victim; the death function 0x45DA20 calls it after the experience award for a victim of type 16 whose death script point 12 did not veto and whose flag 0x1 is clear, with the killer's PC found through the leader chain 0x45DDA0, which walks several levels; the killer handle is the context's +0x30, the injury's attacker the context's +0x8, both set to the attacker by 0x4B2210): the PC's log, the victim's name by 0x45F970(victim, PC) (field 0x17 when the victim is the PC or remembers it, else 0xF0), the total, then the victim's stats 0x11, 0x13 and 0x10 against the slots as LB21 says. The session feeds both from its attack steps: the hero's critical self-hit and an NPC's hit on the hero (injuries), the death of an NPC dealt by the hero or an NPC whose leader is the hero (kill, after the experience award), with the victim's description numbers from its fields (the new `CombatNpcFields.UnknownDescription`, slot 58), the stored alignment clamped to ±1000 and the aptitude derived from its stored points (its effects are not read). The hero's critical self-hit on an object is fed too. Not fed: a death or injury by spell damage (the session's spell damage step is not wired to the log; it does carry attacker and killer), burning, a trap or a script (no attacker), a follower's own death by a critical self-hit (the original probably credits its PC leader; not read), a follower of a follower as killer (the session tests the direct leader only) and the dying script's veto (point 12). The session's scar limit (64) does not stop a scar record, the injury log is capped (2000, project policy), and a cause below 1 records nothing (the original would write a zero number and cut its own list: original bug, not reproduced). | Original (the zero-cause cut list and the unchecked overrun: original bugs, not reproduced) |
| LB23 | create 0x53F0E0 (0x53F263..0x53F2BA), view-window mode 0x550DA0 with its point test 0x551000 and refresh 0x551080, window callback 0x53F350 (mouse branch, button branches), message getter 0x4D43A0, hint post 0x550750, leave 0x550720 (ARC-UI-098) | The create, after the tab group, centres the game view on the logbook's object (the local PC): its location (0x406DA0, field 2) goes to 0x4B8CE0, as the inventory's open does (IW33); then it turns on the view-window mode 1 (0x550DA0) with the window, the table's rectangle 0x5C3418 = (25, 24, 89, 89) and art 198, which is the mode the inventory's round view uses: every refresh of the game view copies the game window's square of 178 × 178 at (311, 96) (centre 15 pixels above the middle of the 800 × 400 view) into the rectangle at half size and draws the art over it (IW33), and the destroy (0x53F2F0) turns the mode off (0x550DA0 with 0). Unlike the inventory open there is no main-menu test. The callback has two branches. A mouse event (type 0) is acted on only for subtype 1, a left **release** (subtype 0 is the press, as in IW34; the LB14 wording "press" was a slip): the point is tested against the lens (0x551000: the window's origin plus the rectangle, left and top edges in, right and bottom out, a square test, the round art is not consulted); inside it the window closes through 0x53F090 (LB2, the close sound 3009) and the event is consumed; outside it nothing happens in this branch. A button event (type 1): subtype 0 on a tab button switches the tab (0x53F5F0, LB14), subtype 1 on the previous or next button turns the page (LB15), subtype 2 is the pointer entering a button and subtype 3 leaving it. Entering one of the seven tab buttons (found by the id in the table, the index is the tab index) fetches message *tab index* of `mes\logbk_ui.mes` (0x4D43A0 on the file kept at 0x648984, the same message the tab's title shows, LB17) and posts it as a type 6 text (0x550750), the world map's button hint path (WM61, without the "Action" header): shown in the message window only when at least 3000 ms passed since the last stored message and the style is 0, else dropped; never stored. Leaving a tab button calls the clear 0x550720 (WM60: acts in style 0 only). The page buttons have no hint. | Original (read on 2026-11-08 in 1.0.7.4, SHA-256 `9bd80511…3678d52`, by this task, agreeing with LB14's first reading and with IW33, IW34 and WM60 / WM61, whose paths the lens and the hint share; the hint's behaviour after the window is destroyed with the pointer on a tab is not read: Steamwoven clears it) |

Not modelled by Steamwoven: the order of equal times (Steamwoven orders them by number), a rumor learned at game time 0
(the original's zero time means not known; no profile starts there), the developer quotes page, the
other tabs still to build (their builders 0x4C4200 and 0x4C3D50, 0x4F5590 and 0x4F5770, 0x463110 are the later slices' to read), the
logbook button's highlight (the PC lens, its click and the tab hover hint are built by ARC-UI-098, LB22). ARC-UI-074 (LB14–LB17, read on
2026-11-07 by a first reader and an independent second reader that saw only the questions) draws the book pages, the seven tab buttons, the
titles and page numbers and the entries' colours, and takes the month names, state labels and titles from `logbk_ui.mes` when the game folder has it;
the pixel height of a wrapped entry is the viewer's (its line count times the font's tallest glyph), where the original measures the text with its own text library.

## Reading window (ARC-UI-049)

Source: the [reading window runtime](../../world/reading-window-runtime.md). Question: how does the original show a written item
(books, notes, newspapers, telegrams, pictures, plaques), which texts and art it reads, how it pages, opens and closes, which
sounds it plays, what state it changes, and who else opens it? Read on 2026-10-21 by this task and, independently, by a fresh agent
that saw only the questions (method step 6); where they differed (the markup, the buffer size, the filler wrap, the alternate art,
the masthead) a third reading settled the row. The rows are F2 unless marked. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. Prototype
numbers below are the project's (the executable's basic number + 20: its 14072 is our 14092).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WR1 | module init (0x56BAA0) | Loads `mes\gamebook.mes`, `gamenote.mes`, `gamenewspaper.mes`, `gametelegram.mes` and `gameplaque.mes`, and counts the newspaper fillers with 0x4D4550(10, 999): 0 when message 10 is missing, else the messages present from 10 on, up to 999. | Original |
| WR2 | reading start (0x56BB60) → 0x4EE290 → open (0x56BC00) | A dead or null reader returns; subtype 5 registers (SK2); otherwise a flag is set for prototype 14092 and 0x4EE290 continues only for the local PC (an NPC reader gets nothing). The open toggles: a second open closes. A missing text file of subtypes 0–3 and 6 refuses silently. The interface mode change pops the current mode (an open inventory closes) and pushes the reading mode 15; leaving mode 15 closes the window (0x551D3D → 0x56BC90). | Original |
| WR3 | create (0x56BCC0), kinds and art | Book 0 → background art 477, note 1 → 489, newspaper 2 → 487, telegram 3 → 491, picture 4 → the start line is the art, plaque 6 → 633; frame art 494. The prototype-14092 flag selects art 817 for every subtype but a picture. Neither the subtype (an index into the art table) nor a picture's art is range-checked. | Original (WRA1, WRA2) |
| WR4 | create (0x56BCC0), body | The messages from the item's first text line (field 203) on, up to ten, stopping at the first missing number. The last text line (field 204) and the written flags (201) are never read. | Original (WRD2) |
| WR5 | book (0x56BCC0, 0x56C050) | Two pages of 200 × 290 at (227, 57) and (476, 57) with the fonts of the markup; buttons with arts 495 and 496 turn two pages back or on; the page tables hold 100 entries and are filled without a bound; the book always opens at its first spread. | Original (WRA4) |
| WR6 | note | The messages joined, each followed by a newline, in a 20,000-byte buffer appended without a bound; font 497; no markup and no paging. | Original (WRA3) |
| WR7 | telegram | The form texts 1000–1010 and 1011 as headers; each body message followed by message 1012, in the same buffer; font 480; no markup. | Original (WRA3) |
| WR8 | newspaper masthead (0x56C050) | Six header entries: messages 0, 1, 0, 2, 3, 2 with fonts 485, 484, 485, 482, 482, 482; with the prototype-14092 flag messages 0, 4, 0, 2, 5, 2 and the second font 815. The headline is the start line; the next nine fill four columns. | Original |
| WR9 | newspaper filler (0x56C050) | After the body the columns are filled from message 10 + start / 10 (10 when that is at least 10 + the filler count), each next message one higher, wrapping to 10 at 10 + the count; a missing message stops the fill. | Original |
| WR10 | plaque | The markup applies; one text rectangle; white text. | Original |
| WR11 | markup (0x56C9F0) | The font is `atoi` of the message (leading whitespace and a sign skipped); a loop then skips the digits only; the next character is tested for `c`/`C` (centred) and is dropped whatever it is; then one whitespace character more is dropped. A font outside the art range is not checked. | Original; Steamwoven switch (WRD3, ARC-TECHNOLOGY-009) |
| WR12 | sounds and close (0x56BC00, 0x56BC90, the window handler) | Open plays 3008, a book's page turn 3010, close 3009. The window closes by an 89 × 89 hotspot at (25, 24) of the frame, by any interface mode change or by a second open; its handler takes no key. | Original |
| WR13 | item use (0x462CC0, variant 0x462FC0) | The item's use script (attachment point 1) runs first and can cancel the use; an item with a spell mana store or the magical item flag (0x4) takes the item-spell path instead; in a turn-based round the use costs 4 action points, whose result is not tested; then the reading start (WR2). | Original |
| WR14 | state | Reading sets no read flag and changes nothing besides the schematic registration (SK2). | Original |
| WR15 | script actions (0x447220) | Action 64 shows a book (number, viewer), 65 a picture (art, viewer), 114 a plaque (number, viewer): only a player viewer gets the window, with no dead check. Action 94 sets a written item's first text line. | Original (94 not implemented) |
| WR16 | the world | No path reads scenery in place; a plaque on a map is shown by a script that calls action 114 (use, tile or sector scripts). | Original (F1: one reader, by the absence of another caller) |

Anomalies. Crash class (the no-crash rule; both settings fixed):

- WRA1 (WR3): a subtype outside 0..6 reads past the art table. Both settings refuse the item (`SubtypeOutOfRange`; the use is refused as an invalid item).
- WRA2 (WR3): a picture art outside 0..4095 draws a handle out of range. Both settings refuse it (`ArtOutOfRange`); a markup font outside the range draws font 0.
- WRA3 (WR6, WR7): the note and telegram buffer is appended without a bound. Both settings stop at 19,999 bytes. No installed document comes near it.
- WRA4 (WR5): a book of more than 100 pages writes past the page tables. Both settings stop at 100 pages.

Data oddities (not crash class; both settings follow the original until the owner decides; a proposal each; WRD3 decided below):

- WRD1 (WR4): four template prototypes (14082, 14083, 14085, 14089) start at line 0, where no message exists: their window is empty. 14089
  gets its line from its insert-item script (action 94, not implemented); the newspapers 14084 and 14092 at line 0 show the masthead
  messages as their body. Proposal: keep (the items as placed on maps or given by scripts carry their own lines).
- WRD2 (WR4): the last text line is 0 in every written prototype (spans −1050..0) and the original never reads it. Proposal: keep.
- WRD3 (WR11): a message without a font prefix loses its first character (two installed documents have a font-0 message). **Decided**
  (owner, 2026-10-21): a switch, `WrittenRuleProfile.UnprefixedMessageLosesFirstCharacter` (ARC-TECHNOLOGY-009): Steamwoven reads a
  message whose first character is not a digit as having no markup (its whole text, font 0, not centred); Original keeps the drop.

Not modelled (named in the runtime): the item-spell path, the 4 action points in a round, action 94, the original's pixel paging
and fonts. The use script before a reading runs since ARC-SCRIPT-020 (IU rows below); the two readable prototypes with one (14086,
14090) are read.

## Save list (ARC-UI-010)

Source: the [save list](../../world/game-session-runtime.md#save-list-arc-ui-010), the
[save and load windows](../../rendering/terrain-viewer.md#save-list-arc-ui-010) and
[FR-LIFECYCLE-002](../../requirements/ui-media-lifecycle.md#fr-lifecycle-002). Question: what F7 and F8 save and load,
how the save windows order, number, overwrite and delete saves, and what the preview is. Read on 2026-10-27 by this task
and, independently, by a fresh agent that saw only the questions (method step 6). They first disagreed on which argument
of the list sort picks the comparator; the second reader recounted the stack offsets and agreed with the first, so every
row below is agreed. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The rows are F2.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SL1 | in-game key handler (0x401560), F7 branch | F7 (scancode 0x41) does nothing for a dead PC; closes an open world map first; refuses with a message in turn-based combat when another critter has the turn; otherwise saves through the game library save (0x403030) under the slot code `SlotAuto` with the description "Auto-Save". There is no other automatic save: the save function's only other callers are the save window and a debug export key. | Original (not modelled: the dead-PC refusal, the other-turn refusal (in the viewer's round, ARC-COMBAT-020, an NPC's subturn ends at once, so the hero has the turn whenever F7 is read) and the world-map close (the open world map owns F7); the viewer adds its own refusals: reset, transfer, cast) |
| SL2 | F8 branch, quick load (0x543220), latest save (0x403850), list sort (0x403C10, comparators 0x403D40, 0x403DB0) | F8 (0x42) loads the save whose `.gsi` file was modified last, the auto-save or any numbered slot: the latest-save helper lists every `save\slot*.gsi` and sorts by modification time, newest first, without pinning the auto-save. An empty list does nothing. The load checks the save's version (25) and shows a success or failure message. The earlier F0 reading of F8 as "the auto slot" was a slip ([keyboard](../../requirements/original-keyboard-mouse.md) open row). | Reconstruction slip |
| SL3 | save window open (0x543380), load list, delete refresh | Both windows list the saves with the auto-save first (an entry whose fifth character is `A`), then newest first by file time. The save window adds a "new save" row 0 and selects row 1 (the first save) when there is one, else nothing. | Original (Steamwoven selects the new-save row in an empty window so Return saves) |
| SL4 | save action (0x5435D0), new-save row | A new save takes the highest slot number plus one: the list is sorted by name, descending, the auto-save skipped, and the number after `Slot` parsed; with no numbered save the name is `Slot0000`. A result of 9999 or more aborts without a message. | Original |
| SL5 | save action (0x5435D0), listed row; description | Saving onto a listed save (the auto-save included) asks first (message 5064, yes/no); before asking, the field is set to that save's own description, so an overwrite keeps it. An empty or all-space description is replaced by the slot name; other text is kept as typed (leading spaces too). Success shows message 5002 and closes the window, failure 5003. | Original |
| SL6 | delete in the load window (0x543160) and the save window (0x544320), file delete (0x403790) | Delete or Backspace or the button asks first (message 5150), then deletes the save's `.gsi`, `.bmp`, `.tfaf` and `.tfai`, reloads and re-sorts the list (auto-save first, newest first) and clears the selection. The file delete refuses the auto-save's file name `SlotAutoAuto-Save` (slot code plus its "Auto-Save" description): nothing happens, no message. | Original (Steamwoven protects the auto slot whatever its description) |
| SL7 | save window keys (0x546EE0) | Up/Down select, Delete/Backspace delete, Return confirms, Escape closes (U3 of the keyboard requirements). | Original |
| SL8 | save info and preview (0x403DD0, 0x404010), sizes (0x4020F0, 0x4044A0 from the main-menu module init 0x540930) | A save records its module, PC name, map, game date, portrait, level, location, story state, description and a preview written as `save\<slot>.bmp`. The preview buffer is first set to a quarter of the game window each way, then, at the main-menu module's start, to a fixed 468×300; the game view is blitted into it (whether the blit scales was not read). | Original (Steamwoven fits the screen into 468×300 and stores a PNG) |
| SL9 | F7 branch of the key handler (0x401560 → 0x5496E0, 0x403030, 0x549750), message note 0x5496F0 | F7 posts a note before the save (`mes\MainMenu.mes` 5060, "Saving..."; the texts quoted in SL9–SL11 are read from the data archive, not from the executable) and one after it (5062, "Save completed."). A note is a type 6 entry of the message window (ML1, ML2): drawn only in the message mode and only when no entry was stored in the last 3 s, never stored, with no sound. The save function posts no message of its own; its result is ignored, so the completion note follows a failed save too. The turn-based refusal posts 5065 (0x549760, not modelled, SL1). | Original; the completion note after a failed save is a technical display bug: fixed in both rule modes (ARC-DEV-168 posts the save-failure result 5003 instead) |
| SL10 | load (0x5432B0; callers: the load window's action 0x5422C0 at 0x54231B, F8's quick load 0x543220 at 0x54326F, Last Save 0x544440 at 0x544585), F8 branch (0x549770, 0x549780) | Whoever asks, a load that reads posts a type 4 entry (stored, message-window sound 3004, ML3, ML5) with 5000 ("Game Loaded Successfully."); a version other than 25 posts 5001 at once, and a save that does not read (the load runs on the version-25 path after a reset of the interface, and resets it again) posts it after that; both post 5001 ("Save Game Corrupt!  Load Failed!") the same way and returns failure. F8 also posts notes: 5061 ("Loading...") before and 5063 ("Load completed.") after, both whatever the outcome; as the result was stored a moment before, the 3 s rule holds the second note back. F8 with no save at all, or whose info does not read, posts only the two notes (no result). | Original |
| SL11 | save window action (0x5435D0 → 0x403030) | A save from the window posts a type 4 entry: 5002 ("Game Saved Successfully.") when it wrote, 5003 ("Save Game Corrupt!  Save Failed!") when it failed (SL5, SF4). The window's save closes the menu on success only. | Original (the viewer's own refusals of a save (a cast, a missile in flight, a transfer, a map load) or of a load (a reset, a map load) post nothing: the original has none; the one refusal it has, F7 in turn-based combat on another critter's turn (5065, SL9), is not modelled. The viewer has one save file, not the original's info file plus archive, so a damaged file posts 5001 after F8, where the original's info-read failure posts nothing) |
| SL12 | F7 over an open window: key fetch 0x52BDA0 → window system 0x51E790, inventory handler 0x573850 (tail 0x574F9F), overlay window create 0x557230 and its handler install 0x5572D0, key branch 0x4017BC to 0x401859, mode change 0x551A80, leave cases 0x551E90 (entry 11 at 0x551CB5), inventory close 0x573440 (ARC-DEV-323, one reading, F1) | The fetch offers every record to the window system, and what a window consumes never reaches the loop (a record type 6 tick is not consumed). The inventory handler serves the overlay window of every inventory mode (8, 4, 10, 11, 19, 20); a key record (type 5) matches none of its branches and falls to the common tail, which answers 0 unless the window asks for its own close (a partner test in the barter kind) or an engine flag is set, so **a key reaches the main loop with the steal window open**; the overlay window's create call passes the handler flag 0x2 alone (the window system's consume-all bit is 0x4; the copy into the window table was not traced, but Escape and the window keys reach the main loop over the same window, SA45). The F7 branch (on the key release) then runs its own tests (a dead hero: nothing; the world map is closed first; in a round when the acting critter is not the hero: note 5065; a network game: nothing), calls the mode change with 0 **twice** (0x401834, 0x40183B), writes the "Saving..." note, saves ("SlotAuto", "Auto-Save") and writes the completion note. With the stack [0, 11] the first pop runs the leave case of the steal mode, which is the inventory close (0x573440) unless the mode being entered is the quantity picker (13), then a held item is put back (0x575770); the second pop is a pop at the base: the depth stays, mode 0's leave and enter cases run again (a refresh). So F7 **saves over the open steal window and closes it first**; nothing of the window is saved (the interface and the window's globals are not a module of the save). The same table gives modes 8 and 10 the same close (not built, SL12 in the viewer is the steal window only); the dialog's leave case ends the conversation (0x5678D0) and the dialog's key hook (SV8 Q1) is not read for F7. | Original (both rule modes, owner decision D9 card 2026-11-05: interface input as the original; ARC-DEV-323 builds it for the steal window: F7 over it closes it and saves) |
| SL13 | inventory close 0x573440 and what it reaches (ARC-DEV-323, F1) | The close clears the window-open flag, hides the window (0x4A53B0), runs the container branch only for the window kinds 2 and 4 (loot kinds, with a container holder; the steal window is kind 3, so not for steal), redraws (0x550DA0, 0x579D70, 0x557330, 0x551160) and pops the mode stack by one or two (an overlay over the inventory goes too). Followed through its callees to depth 5, **no call reaches a goal builder or the goal table's interrupt or end routines** (0x44E2C0, 0x44E4D0, 0x424070, 0x44D4E0); deeper they are reached only through generic hubs (hover text, the script interpreter, object destroy, the held-item put-back 0x575770), all conditional and data-driven, about 1900 indirect calls unresolved (a second reading by the reviewer). So closing the steal window (F7, Escape, the I key, a click on the close area, another window's key) **ends no goal**: goal 33 (the walk to the victim) and goal 86 (the attempt) go on, and the attempt of node 4 is made with the window closed, as after a load (GT204). The viewer's earlier choice (ARC-DEV-184: the window's close ends the run) was not read from the executable (GT144 left the close path untraced) and is replaced. | Original (reconstruction slip of ARC-DEV-184: both rule modes follow the original, no switch) |

The original's file layout (`.gsi` metadata, `.tfaf`/`.tfai` archives) is not read or written: Steamwoven keeps its own
snapshot, details and PNG files with the same list rules.

## Main menu (ARC-UI-011)

Source: the [main menu](../../rendering/terrain-viewer.md#main-menu-arc-ui-011),
[FR-UI-001](../../requirements/ui-media-lifecycle.md#fr-ui-001) and
[FR-LIFECYCLE-001](../../requirements/ui-media-lifecycle.md#fr-lifecycle-001). Question: which windows and buttons the
original's main menu has before play, what their texts and keys are, how a button and Escape move between windows, and
what "Last Save" does. Read on 2026-10-28 by this task and, independently, by a fresh agent that saw only the claims
(method step 6). The second reader corrected two claims of the first (the "back" target is -2, not -1; Escape on the
root window needs the counter at 2 or more and then exits the game); the rows below are the agreed reading. Every direct
branch stays inside `.text`. The rows are F2.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MM1 | window table (0x5C3A40), window open (0x541740), close and back (0x5417A0), back stack (0x5417E0, 0x541810) | The menu is a table of pointers to window descriptors indexed by the current window type. A descriptor names its background art, open and close functions, the first message of its labels in `mes\MainMenu.mes`, its button count and its buttons; a button names its position, art, target window (-2 goes back, 0 exits) and hotkey. Opening a window pushes the current one on a back stack; back closes the current window and opens the one below it. | Original (Steamwoven keeps the windows, buttons and back stack, not the art or positions) |
| MM2 | window 2, 5 and 11 descriptors | Window 2 (main): Single Player, MultiPlayer, Options, Credits, Exit Game (messages 10–14), opening windows 5, 16, 6, 25 and exiting. Window 5 (single player): New Game, Load Game, Last Save, View Intro, Cancel (50–54), opening 11, 7, 9, 10 and going back. Window 11 (new game): Pick Character, New Character, Cancel (420–422), opening 13 (the pre-made browser, 0x545870) and 12 (the character editor, 0x5446F0) and going back. | Original; Steamwoven hides MultiPlayer, Credits, View Intro and New Character (not built; FR-UI-001 shows only what is supported) and names them in the window; Options opens the options window since ARC-UI-013 (OP1) |
| MM3 | window build (0x546340) | A button's label is message base + i; its hotkey is the first character of message base + 10 + i, upper-cased, filled in when the window is built (-1 and a log line when the message is missing). | Original |
| MM4 | Last Save (0x544440), latest save (0x403850), save info (0x404270), load (0x5432B0) | Window 9 looks up the newest save; with none it goes back to the window it came from without a message; otherwise it reads the save's info and, when that reads, shows "Loading Save Game..." (message 5) and loads it; an info failure returns without loading. | Original; Steamwoven also names "no save to continue from" in the window, and shows a failed load's reason |
| MM5 | View Intro (0x5445F0) | Plays movies 1 and 7, then returns to window 5 (fixed, not from the back stack). | Not built (no movie playback, FR-MEDIA-001) |
| MM6 | menu event handler (0x546EE0), key table (0x547DBC, index 0x547DE0) | A typed character is upper-cased and activates the first enabled button with that hotkey. Escape (scancode 1) goes back in windows 5, 11, 12 and 13. On window 2 Escape acts only when the menu's open counter (0x64C43C, only ever incremented, by window 2's open function) is 2 or more, that is when the main menu is open again after its first opening, and then exits the game as Exit Game does. | Original for the windows Steamwoven has: Escape on the root does nothing at the menu's first opening and exits once it opens again after a quit (ARC-UI-012, SF3) |
| MM7 | button activation (mouse 0x54736C, hotkey 0x547BAD) | A button with a target above 0 runs the window's veto callback, closes the current window and opens the target; -2 goes back; 0 closes the menu and exits the game. | Original |

The pre-made start is the existing ARC-WORLD-027 reading of 0x545B60 ([CC-07](../character-creation-evidence.md)); the start
place is the map list's start of the start map (runtime map 1), where 0x5412E0 teleports a new game's PC. Steamwoven
makes the new game's session as a fresh launch makes it (the module's clock, the map's start cell, its reputations) with
the picked hero; the original's movies 1 and 7, fade, newspaper event and random-encounter schedule at the start are not
modelled.

## Scene flow (ARC-UI-012)

How the original goes between play and its main menu: the in-play menu that Escape opens, its buttons, the quit to the
main menu and the first opening at start. Read on 2026-10-28 by this task and, independently, by a fresh agent that saw
only the questions (method step 6); the second reader agreed and added the Return and Escape answers of the quit box,
the window-map and dialog flags of the short window and the start's skip of window 0; the rows below are the agreed
reading. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The rows are F2.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SF1 | menu start (0x541180), main loop Escape (near 0x401705), window 3 and 4 descriptors | Escape in play (no flag of the dialog window 0x67B964 or the world map 0x66D864 set, single player, and in turn-based combat only on the PC's own turn) starts the menu with type 2: window 3, labels 30–34 (Save Game, Load Game, Options, Quit, Continue; Save opens window 8, Load window 7, Options window 6, the last two go to the window's callback). Otherwise type 3: window 4, labels 70–72 (Options, Quit, Continue). Starting the menu pauses the game clock. Window 26 (labels from 30, Save, Options and two callback buttons) is started by no caller in this version. | Original (both rule modes, ARC-DEV-111): window 4 opens in a dialog, on the world map, in a barter (its dialog is still active, inference) and on another combatant's turn, and no window takes Escape itself; Options opens the options window (ARC-UI-013, OP1); window 26 is not built |
| SF2 | in-play callback (0x541B50, window 4: 0x541BE0), menu close (0x5412D0, 0x5412E0), Escape on window 3 (key table entry 8, 0x547A96) | Continue closes the menu and play resumes (the clock's pause ends). Escape on window 3 (and 4) pops the back stack, which holds nothing below it when opened from play, so it closes the menu the same way. | Original |
| SF3 | quit question (0x5416A0), quit to the main menu (0x549310), module load (0x402A50), interface reset (0x53E730, 0x541710) | Quit asks a yes/no box with message 5100 of `mes\MainMenu.mes` (letters Y and N, either case; Return or the first button is yes, Escape or the second is no). On yes the menu's windows close, the back stack empties, the game's modules are unloaded (each subsystem's unload in reverse order), the default module "Arcanum" is loaded again (emptying `Save\Current`; saved games are not touched and nothing is saved) and the interface is reset; the main menu (window 2) opens again, so its open counter reaches 2 and its Escape exits the game (MM6). When the module does not load the game logs "MainMenu: Unable to load default module" and exits. | Original; Steamwoven loads the start scene from the launch arguments with a fresh session instead of a module reload, and keeps the failed scene on screen instead of exiting |
| SF4 | save action (0x5435D0), save (0x403030) | A save that succeeds posts message 5002 and closes the menu (play resumes); a failed save posts 5003 and the save window stays. | Original (ARC-UI-010's save window already closes on success; under the in-play menu the menu closes with it) |
| SF5 | main-menu interface init (0x540930), menu start type 0, first menu step (0x541590), main loop menu flag (0x53EAD0, 0x63CBE0) | At start the main-menu interface's init starts the menu with type 0, which opens the empty window 0; the first menu step sees window 0 and at once opens the main window 2. The main loop starts the menu again with type 0 only after a quit ran while the menu was not open (0x53EAF0 sets the flag). The game's death and end-game resets also restart the menu with type 0. | Original; Steamwoven opens the main menu directly; the death reset is ARC-UI-026's (PD8), the end-game reset a later task |

## Hero death (ARC-UI-026)

What the original does after the PC's death in single player, from the dying animation to the main menu. Read on
2026-10-28 by this task and, independently, by a fresh agent that saw only the questions (method step 6); the second
reader agreed on every row and added the dying goal's identity, the time-event clock, the release-only input and the
gamma fade's step time (PD9), which this task then confirmed. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The rows are F2.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PD1 | dying goal's last node (0x42FF40, goal 31 "ag_dying"), UI death callback (0x460520 → 0x57CDC0, registered by 0x57C6E0 through 0x45FD40) | When the dying animation of an object ends and the object is dead (0x45D8D0), it gets flags 0x404; in single player, when the object is the PC, the interface's death callback runs; every dead PC or NPC then fires its worn items' death triggers (0x4CBC60). | Original; the viewer plays no dying animation for the hero, so Steamwoven starts the wait when the session's hero becomes dead; a session whose hero is dead already (a load) starts nothing |
| PD2 | death callback (0x57CDC0), UI event (0x57D370), time-event table (0x5B2188, entry 2), scheduling (0x45B800, 0x45BA30), save (0x45AED0) | The callback queues the interface's time event (type 2, sub-code 10) 5000 ms later. Type 2 runs on the real-time clock (time base 0, which advances also while the game time is paused; each ping is capped at 250 ms) and is not saved, so a save and load during the wait drops it. | Original (real time; the viewer's frame time) |
| PD3 | interface event handler (0x57D410, sub-code 10 at 0x57D465), inventory close (0x573620, 0x575770), menu flag (0x541680), menu close (0x5412D0) | When the event comes it checks the PC again: alive, nothing happens. Then an open inventory window (with its loot and barter modes) closes; when a main-menu window is open it closes and the event is queued again 300 ms later (the checks repeat). | Original (the viewer also closes its barter window, which the original's inventory window holds) |
| PD4 | slide show (0x569600), script call (0x569720, table 0x5A5700), queue (0x569A90, 0x569AA0) | The show (type 1 for a death) clears its queue and runs script 998 (997 for the end game, 999 for the main menu's credits window), which may queue slides (up to 100). For a death only, when nothing was queued and `rules\slide.mes` is loaded, slide 0 is queued. The installed data has no script 998. | Original; Steamwoven runs no slide script, so a death always shows slide 0 when the table reads |
| PD5 | slide table load (0x5695B0, interface module load loop 0x53E730), pair lookup (0x569930) | `rules\slide.mes` is read at module load. A slide's message is split with `strtok` at space, tab and line feed: the first word is the picture `slide\<word>`, the second the voice `sound\speech\slide\<word>`; a missing message or second word is no slide. The installed message 0 names `death.bmp` and `DeathSound.mp3`. | Original |
| PD6 | slide show (0x569600), music (0x41C340, 0x41C450), window (0x51CB90) | The show always fades to black first. With slides queued it opens an 800 × 600 window, stops the music, hides the pointer and loops `sound\music\DwarvenMusic.mp3` at the music volume; it shows the slides in queue order, stopping early when one returns 0; then it stops the music and closes the window. A death or end-game show leaves the screen black. | Original (the viewer stops every scene sound under the screen) |
| PD7 | one slide (0x569770) | A slide without a pair is skipped. A picture that does not load shows nothing: the slide drains the input and fades out. Otherwise the picture fades in, its voice starts, the input queued so far is dropped, and the slide waits: a key's release ends it, Escape's release also ends the show; a left, right or middle button release (mouse events 1, 3 and 5) ends it; presses do nothing. Without a voice the slide ends at once; it also ends when the voice ends. Then the voice stops, the input is drained and the slide fades out. | Original |
| PD8 | death reset (0x57D465 on), game reset (0x402380), interface reset (0x53E6E0), menu start (0x541180 type 0), menu step (0x541590 from the main loop near 0x401EF3), fade (0x4BDFA0) | After the show the game logs "DEATH: Resetting game!", resets the game (emptying `Save\Current`; the module stays loaded) and the interface, starts the menu with type 0 and fades in (2 s). The main loop's next menu step sees window 0 and opens the main window 2 at once, whose open counter now makes its Escape exit the game (MM6). No window offers a choice; a load is the main menu's. The end game (sub-code 11) does the same without the death check, with show type 0. | Original; Steamwoven loads the start scene from the launch arguments with a fresh session as its quit does (SF3). The Steamwoven setting first asks whether to load the newest save (an owner addition, ARC-UI-028, switch `LifecycleRuleProfile.DeathResetsToMainMenu`); Original resets at once |
| PD9 | fade (0x4BDFA0), gamma fade (0x51FCA0), fallback | Every fade here passes 48 steps and 2.0 s. With gamma support the gamma fade waits `_ftol(2.0 / 48)` = 0 ms per step, so the 48 ramps run back to back and the fade flashes by; without it the fallback fills the screen black and waits 2000 ms in both directions. | A bug of the original (a units slip, technical): fixed in both rule modes, the fades take 2 s ([decision](../../architecture/decisions.md)) |

fresh agent that saw only the questions (method step 6). The readers agree on every row; the second reader added that
the window's letter hotkeys are not acted on (OP7, checked again by the first reader), the display quirks of Auto
Attack and Always Run (OP4) and Brightness's gamma range (OP2). Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The rows are F2.

"auto switch" is never registered: it reads 0 until its setter writes it. Default Combat Mode 0 is real time (turn-based and fast off), 1 turn-based, 2 fast turn-based (both on). ## Inventory window (ARC-UI-015)

The player's own inventory window (mode 0 of the inventory open): how it opens and closes, what a press, a release and a right
click do with an item, the swap on an occupied doll slot, the drop zones, action points and the weight lines. Source: the
alpha plan's item N6 ([FR-ITEM-005](../../requirements/items-economy.md#fr-item-005),
[FR-UI-004](../../requirements/ui-media-lifecycle.md#fr-ui-004)). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task and, independently, by a fresh
agent that saw only the questions (method step 6). The readers agree on the behaviour; the second reader corrected where the weight lines are drawn and their
label and unit (IW9), added the turn-based key filter (IW1), the held item's hidden flag (IW2) and where a drop goes (IW6), and
the first reader checked these corrections again. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The rows are F2. The
window's texts come from `mes\inven_ui.mes`. Event types and subtypes are the engine's (event 0 = mouse: subtype 0 left press,
1 left release, 2 right press, 3 right release, 6 move, 7 a still pointer; HK15). These rows first named subtype 3 a right press;
ARC-UI-032 read the handler's branch again (0x574248..0x5742A7): it is the right release, and a right press (subtype 2) does nothing in
the window.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IW1 | key handler I branch (0x54B9B9), open (0x572240 → 0x572340, 0x572370, 0x572510, 0x572640, 0x5727B0), close (0x573440) | I opens mode 0 for the local PC with no partner. An open window closes instead; an item held on the cursor is first put back (0x575770). Only a null, dead (0x45D8D0) or non-PC holder refuses (BT4). Mode 0 sets interface mode 8, and the mode change (0x551A80) never refuses: the old mode's window closes (dialog, logbook, character sheet, reading, schematic; SW3). Neither the open nor the I branch tests combat, so the window opens in real-time and turn-based combat; in a turn-based round the in-game filter (0x54B5D0) drops every key but Space while another critter has the turn (CT6). The close returns the interface to mode 0. A network client only sends a request. | Original; Steamwoven's window is modal and opens only when no other window or menu owns the input (the I key is ignored there instead of closing that window) |
| IW2 | handler 0x573850, left press (subtype 0) | In the inventory interface modes (8, 10, 11, 4) with nothing held, the item under the cursor (grid cell or worn slot, 0x575FA0; an item with object flag 0x2 or item flag 0x10 is skipped) goes onto the cursor; the grab's cell offset within the item is kept. The item stays in its list and slot until it is released, marked with item flag 0x10 (0x5754C0), which the redraw skips, so it looks gone. No check runs on the press. | Original; Steamwoven has buttons instead of a cursor (not built) |
| IW3 | left release (subtype 1) on a worn slot (0x575CB0 locations 1000..1008), 0x464D20, 0x4649C0, 0x466DA0, 0x466E00, 0x466510, 0x466640, move 0x5788C0 | The wear check (0x464D20) for that slot and holder runs first; a refusal shows its message (0x4673F0) and the item goes back. An empty slot, or the item's own: the move (IW4). An occupied slot: the occupant's remove check (0x466DA0: its no-drop flag refuses, with the message); then both leave their places and the occupant's insert check into the PC's list runs with both out (0x466510); a refusal shows its message and puts both back. Otherwise the item goes back to its place and then moves into the slot (IW4, whose own no-drop refusal leaves the slot empty), the occupant goes into the grid cell the check found while the item was out, and the occupant is now held on the cursor. | Original; Steamwoven's *Wear* button runs the same checks in the session (`Equip`, EQ5) and the occupant goes back to the pack (no cursor) |
| IW4 | move 0x5788C0, same holder; 0x466DA0, 0x466310, 0x466390, 0x465010 | A move within the PC's inventory: the remove check (0x466DA0) refuses an item with the no-drop flag, with its message, for every move (also grid to grid); the worn-slot check (0x465010) always allows, so taking an item off is never refused by it. Into the grid the target cells must be free of other items, else nothing moves. Then the removal (0x466E00), the insert check and the insert at the new place (0x466640); a refused insert puts the item back where it was, with no message. The counter 0x5E8638 is lowered around the move, so no encumbrance message is shown (WE5). The item's sound plays (0x4F0BF0). | Original; Steamwoven's *Take off* button runs `Unequip` (no grid) |
| IW5 | right release (subtype 3; corrected by ARC-UI-032, first read as a right press), 0x4655D0, 0x462C30, 0x462CC0, 0x4B6D70, 0x4B6D80 | While no hotkey-bar drag is going on (0x54EB50, else the release ends that drop as a left release does) and in mode 0 with nothing held, the item under the pointer at the release: a worn item or a wearable one (wear location not −1) does nothing. Any other item with `OIF_CAN_USE_BOX` (item flag 0x80; 0x462C30 answers 0, else 6) is used (0x462CC0) with the PC as user and target; in turn-based combat only when the PC is the critter whose turn it is, else nothing. | Original; built by ARC-UI-025 (right click or Use button, IW16), on the right release since ARC-UI-032 |

The use icon (x 703, y 331): the use check (0x462C30) refuses and the item goes back; an item with item flag 0x10000 in mode 0 takes the drop path (flag 4); otherwise the item goes back and is used (turn rule of IW5), at the PC with a Shift key held (scancodes 0x2A, 0x36), with no target otherwise. The third icon exists only in mode 1 (barter). On the map outside the window in mode 0 (not while the main menu is up) the held item takes the move with flag 4: its remove check (no-drop refuses), its removal, and the window closes; where the item goes then was not traced by either reader. Other modes put it back. | Original; the drop and use icons built by ARC-UI-025 (IW16, IW17); the map drop is IW18 (built by ARC-UI-063 for the own window) |

## Inventory grid (ARC-UI-019)

The release of a held item on the player's own grid and doll, the same-holder move and the window's frame, for the grid and the drag
of [FR-ITEM-005](../../requirements/items-economy.md#fr-item-005) and [FR-UI-004](../../requirements/ui-media-lifecycle.md#fr-ui-004)
(alpha item N6, part 2; the press is IW2, the wear swap IW3, the grid move IW4). Read on 2026-10-28 by two fresh agents that saw only
the questions (method step 6), each on its own; they agree on every row (one named a cursor hotspot of 2,2 for a swapped occupant,
the other 0,0; nothing here depends on it). Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The rows are F2. Handler 0x573850 runs to
0x574FC5, the move 0x5788C0 to 0x578B75, the hit test 0x575CB0 to 0x575F90. Correction to IW2: 0x5754C0 only queues the pick; in
a single-player game its callback 0x5755A0 runs at once, puts the item on the cursor, sets item flag 0x10 (0x4A50D0) and plays the
pickup sound; 0x4A51C0 clears the flag.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IW10 | release on the grid (0x574B33 → 0x574EB8), 0x575200, move 0x5788C0, 0x466310, 0x466390 | The grab offset is kept as a difference of grid locations (cursor location minus the item's location, row × 10 + column, set at 0x57420B); a worn item's is 0. The target is the cell under the cursor minus the offset, a plain subtraction with no clamping: a column left of the grab column wraps into the row above and fails the width test. A negative target (no cell, or above the first row) tries the hotkey bar (0x57EDA0) and otherwise puts the item back (0x575770: it never left its place; flag cleared, drop sound). Else the move runs: the flag is cleared, the remove check (no-drop refuses with its message and the drop sound, IW4), the item's own cells are cleared from the map (0x466310), and the fit test (0x466390) refuses a rectangle with column + width above 10, row + height above 12 or a taken cell, silently. Then the removal (0x466E00), the insert check (0x466510) and the insertion (0x466640) at the target, or back at the old place when the check refuses, and the drop sound. There is no same-place shortcut: an item released on its own cell or a worn item on its own slot takes the whole move. After every grid release the cursor is empty (0x574F3A); a refusal leaves the item where it was. | Original |
| IW11 | release on a doll slot (0x574B76, 0x574CBA..0x574DC9), 0x4649C0, 0x4664C0 → 0x4675A0, 0x575930 | After the wear check (IW3), an empty slot or the item's own takes the move (IW10, no fit test). An occupied one: the occupant's remove check, both removals, then the insert check of the occupant with both out, whose free slot is the first top-left place, row by row, where the held item's old cells count as free. A refusal shows its message, the occupant goes back into its slot and the held item back to its place (0x575930); the cursor is empty after every refusal. On success the held item is put back at its place and moved into the slot (0x5788C0), the occupant is inserted at the free slot found and becomes the held item there, with offset 0, and the pickup sound plays. | Original |
| IW12 | insert check of a move (0x466510, WE4) | Every move inside the PC's inventory runs the insert check with the item out: the load (the total without the item plus the item) above the carry weight refuses, so a hero over its carry weight can move, wear or take off nothing; then the Technophobia test; then the room: an item going to a worn slot needs a free grid place although it does not take one (a grid item always has one, its own cells). A gold or ammunition stack of the same kind skips the fit test. | Original |
| IW13 | ring slots (0x464D20 → 0x4655D0) | The wear check maps 1002 to 1001 before it compares the ring's own location, so a ring is admitted in either ring slot wherever it is worn now. Into the empty other slot the move takes it out of its slot (1001 ends empty); its insert check needs a free grid place (IW12), so with a full grid the ring silently stays. Onto the other slot holding a ring the swap of IW11 runs: the other ring goes to the first free grid place and onto the cursor, not into the slot the first ring left. | Original; IWA1 (the room test for a ring that only changes slots) is a gameplay bug: Steamwoven moves it without the room test |
| IW14 | hit test 0x575CB0 (modes 0, 5, 6), table 0x5CAC58 | In the player's own window the point's y is lowered by 41; the nine wear-slot rectangles are tested first (x, y, width, height): helmet 151, 107, 64, 64; ring 1 247, 107, 32, 32; ring 2 279, 107, 32, 32; medallion 247, 139, 64, 32; weapon 23, 170, 96, 128; shield 247, 171, 96, 128; armour 119, 171, 128, 160; gauntlets 55, 107, 64, 64; boots 150, 331, 64, 64. Then the grid, 10 columns by 12 rows of 32-pixel cells from (368, 8), all rows with no scroll (other modes scroll 7 or 8 rows). With the window toggle 0x68150C set (buttons at 0x5738A9, 0x5738F9) a second table 0x5CACE8, the same rectangles 348 to the right, is used and no grid cell exists; the toggle's meaning was not read. | Original (frame only; the window's art is not reproduced) |

A right release (subtype 3; first read as a right press, corrected by ARC-UI-032) does nothing while an item is held unless a hotkey-bar drag is active (0x54EB50), when it goes to the release path. Every move fires the item's events through 0x441980 with the holder as triggerer: the removal fires wield off (25) for a worn place, then remove item (28); the insertion fires wield on (24) for a worn place, then insert item (21). Nothing suppresses them for a move inside one holder, also onto the same place; the counter 0x5E8638 only gates the encumbrance message. | Original; Steamwoven runs a grid move's remove-item and insert-item events through the item script host, and moves without such events onto their own place change nothing; a wear move into an empty slot that its insert check refuses runs no events (the original's go out and back in; not built) |

| ID | Behaviour | Classification |
