## Inventory drop and use (ARC-UI-025)

The drop icon, the use icon, the right release (first read as a right press, IW5) and the release on the map in the player's own window, for the drop and use of
[FR-ITEM-005](../../requirements/items-economy.md#fr-item-005) and [FR-UI-004](../../requirements/ui-media-lifecycle.md#fr-ui-004)
(alpha item N6, part 3; IW5, IW6 and IW7 are the earlier readings). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task and, independently, by a fresh agent
that saw only the questions (method step 6); they agree on every row. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The rows are F2. The
transfer request 0x578B80 runs to 0x578E9D, its execution 0x578EA0 to 0x5798DB.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IW16 | drop icon: move 0x5788C0 → request 0x578B80 → 0x578EA0 (0x5793DE..0x5793FA), 0x461A70 | The move to no holder with flag 2 packs a transfer request (flag 2 or 4 clears the source slot) and executes it: an ordinary item goes to 0x461A70, which is the drop 0x461AB0 with distance 0, so the item lands on its holder's tile after the drop's refusals (PI1: the drop script, the no-drop remove check with its message, the tile; then the removal and the placement with its junk pile and decay). Gold or ammunition goes first to the quantity window (IW7); after it the split-off stack is placed on the holder's tile (0x466E50). A network client only sends the request. | Original; Steamwoven's drop icon and Drop buttons run `DropHeroItem`; an ammunition stack takes `DropHeroAmmo` after the quantity window (ARC-UI-029, QW5) |
| IW17 | use icon and right release (IW5): 0x462C30, 0x462CC0 | Both run the use check (`OIF_CAN_USE_BOX`), the turn rule of IW5 and then the item use 0x462CC0 (ledger AG15, TP1, IU1): the right release with the PC as target, the use icon with the PC while Shift is held and with no target otherwise, so an item that needs a target opens the targeting cursor there. On the use icon an item whose use is a throw (`OIF_USE_IS_THROW`, item flag 0x10000) takes the map drop of IW18 instead. | Original; Steamwoven runs `UseItemNow` over the inventory's own world: read, heal with a healing kit, the item's magic through the cast host, spend food and scrolls, 4 action points on the hero's turn; lockpicks' targeting arms the lock pick; another item's targeting cursor and a trap device are not built; ARC-UI-063: the throw's map drop in the own window (IW18) |
| IW18 | release on the round view (0x574559..0x5745F8), 0x551000, 0x578EA0 (0x5792D9..0x57931C, 0x5795E3..0x5795F2), 0x739F58 → 0x574FA5 → 0x573440; game view 0x54DF28..0x54E050 | A left release on the round view (0x551000: the point inside the view window's 89 × 89 rectangle of the window, IW33, no main menu up; corrected by ARC-UI-054, first read as the game view's rectangle) with an item held: in mode 0 the move with flag 4 and no holder; in other modes the item goes back and the window closes. For an ordinary item the request runs the remove check (no-drop refuses, with its message) and the removal (0x466E00 → 0x467860, its wield-off and remove-item events); the item is placed nowhere. On success global 0x739F58 is set and the handler then closes the window. Nothing clears the held item 0x6810E0, so the parentless item stays on the cursor in the game view; there a left press on a tile, with the PC active (0x45D790), clears the cursor (0x573840) and starts the PC's throw goal 37 at that tile (0x434F80); an inactive PC puts the item back (0x575770). Gold or ammunition is split first (IW7): the split-off stack leaves the map (0x4417A0) and goes on the cursor (0x573630) while the rest stays. | Original; built by ARC-UI-063 in both rule modes (the session's throw is ARC-COMBAT-023's): the round view and the use icon lift the item onto the game cursor and close the window, a left press throws it (TT2, TT6), a right release puts it back (TT7). Steamwoven difference: the item stays in the pack or slot until the throw, so a put back only empties the cursor. Ammunition stacks since ARC-UI-086 (QW1 asks, QW5 splits). NOT READY: gold (the hero's gold pile moves in the grid since ARC-UI-090, drops since ARC-UI-091 and lifts and throws since ARC-UI-094 like an ammunition stack), the hotkey bar's lift. The use icon's throw exists in mode 0 only (IW6), so the loot and steal windows have none (ARC-UI-063 follow-up). |
| IW19 | icon rectangles (0x572F4E..0x572F84, 0x577064, 0x5771BC) | The drop icon's corner is (703, 385) and the use icon's (703, 331) on the window, set on open in every mode but 4, 5 and 6 (the icons are hidden there); their width and height are the frame size of their interface art (0xE0; the use icon 0x156, 0x15B while an item is held over it), set on each redraw; they are drawn 41 pixels higher, as the doll and grid frame is (IW14). | Original (frame only; the viewer draws plain 32-pixel squares at those corners) |

The session's drop drops the writes of a drop script when a later refusal (the no-drop flag) keeps the item; the original keeps them
(PI1). This is the same session policy as the pickup's (IE1); no installed item carries a drop script ([item events](../../world/inventory-runtime.md#item-events-arc-script-041)).

Not determined: what the drop rectangle 0x64C660 of the map test (0x551000, IW6) covers, what the other press cursor modes do while
an item is held, and how the grid is drawn.

## Quantity window (ARC-UI-029)

The question how many units of a gold or ammunition stack a move to another holder takes (interface mode 13), for the stacks of
[FR-ITEM-003](../../requirements/items-economy.md#fr-item-003), [FR-ITEM-005](../../requirements/items-economy.md#fr-item-005) and [FR-UI-004](../../requirements/ui-media-lifecycle.md#fr-ui-004)
(alpha item N6, follow-up b; IW7 is the earlier reading). Read on 2026-10-28 by this task and, independently, by a fresh agent that
saw only the questions (method step 6). The readers agree on every row; the second corrected the first on a count of 0 (QW5: the
request returns before any move, so no empty stack is made) and added the cancel by a bar change (QW2). Every direct branch stays
inside `.text`. The rows are F2. Field 0x14F is the item type (6 ammunition, 8 gold); the quantity is field 0xA5 (gold) or 0x8E
(ammunition).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| QW1 | move 0x5788C0, its branch to another holder (0x578A60..0x578B75) | The move to another holder (a drop passes none) stores the transfer request. An item that is not gold or ammunition transfers at once with count 1. A gold or ammunition item is first put back (0x575770: the held item returns to its place, the cursor empties); then its quantity is read (0x462410): one unit transfers at once; the whole stack transfers at once in a loot window (open mode 2) when the move goes into the looted holder (flag 1, the target is the window's other party 0x6814F8), and for Take all (flag 0x20); every other move asks: interface mode 13, bar mode 9, redraw. So a drop, a map drop, a take from a looted holder and both directions of a barter ask. | Original; Steamwoven asks for a dead NPC's gold pile or ammunition stack taken with its Take button and for the hero's ammunition stack dropped on its tile; Take all moves whole stacks (a gold pile too); a junk pile's stacks, the barter window and the hero's gold do not ask yet (not built); the map drop of an ammunition stack asks since ARC-UI-086 |
| QW2 | mode change 0x551A80: entering 13 (0x551DD4), leaving it (0x551CD6), the inventory modes' leave cases (0x551B9D, 0x551C13, 0x551C80, 0x551CB5); 0x5506C0 | Entering mode 13 sets item flag 0x10 (field 0x57) on the requested item, so the window's drawing and hit test skip it, and redraws; the bar mode is set by the caller (9). Leaving it sets bar mode 0, clears the flag and redraws. The inventory modes 4, 8, 10 and 11 keep their window open when the new mode is 13. Choosing any other bar mode while bar 9 is up pops mode 13 first, which cancels the question with no transfer. | Original; Steamwoven's question is a row of the inventory window and leaves the item shown; closing the window cancels it |
| QW3 | bar mode open 0x551210 (case 9, 0x551461), button table 0x5C6D08, counter 0x553960 | Five buttons (x, y, interface art; screen coordinates): All (364, 548; 299), up (479, 547; 805), down (479, 559; 804), Accept (545, 506; 33), Cancel (545, 557; 32). The count (0x64C678) starts at 0; the maximum (0x64C478) is the requested item's quantity (1 for any other item). The kind's icon (gold, or ammunition types 0..3) is drawn at (269, 534), the count right-aligned in 6 digits at (404, 545). Ammunition types 4 and 5 would leave the icon unset (no installed item has them). | Original (frame only; the viewer draws plain buttons and text, no art) |
| QW4 | in-game handler 0x54B5D0 (button events 0x54BCDC, 0x54BE89; key table 0x54C770/0x54C73C; character Enter 0x54C605) | Button event with data 1: All sets the count to the maximum; Accept runs the request with the count (0x578B80) and returns to the previous mode; Cancel only returns. Button event with data 0: up adds 1 below the maximum, down takes 1 above 0. Key presses: the keypad digits 0..9 make count × 10 + digit (32-bit), capped at the maximum; Backspace and Delete divide by 10. The main-row digits and the character table add no digit. The character Enter accepts as the button does. Escape is not handled here. | Original; Steamwoven's buttons act on a click, Enter and the keypad Enter accept, Escape and I cancel (the original's Escape there was not traced), the count is clamped without the 32-bit wrap (above 214 748 364 units) |
| QW5 | request 0x578B80 → 0x578EA0 (0x578F94); gold 0x464830, ammunition 0x4658E0; new objects 0x464970, 0x465A40; 0x4417A0, 0x466E50 | The count goes into the request; a count of 0 returns before any move (the item stays where the put-back left it). Gold moves through 0x464830 and ammunition through 0x4658E0 (with the type, field 0x8F): fewer units than the count refuse, exactly the count deletes the source stack, more leave the rest; a target holder's stack grows or a new one is made (AM4, NT1). A drop (flags 2 or 4, no target) only takes the units; then a new object carries them: gold (0x464970, base prototype 9056) or the ammunition type's base prototype (0x465A40, table 0x5B32B0), with the count as its quantity, at the source holder's location; with flag 2 the drop's placement (0x466E50: junk pile, decay) lays it there, with flag 4 it leaves the map for the cursor (IW18). Neither path runs the item's drop script or its no-drop check (the ordinary item's 0x461A70 and 0x466DA0 do). | Original; Steamwoven's `DropHeroAmmo` (a made stack of the kind the host names), `TakeGoldFromNpc`, `TakeAmmoFromNpc` |
| QW6 | handler 0x573850 (0x574080, 0x5746F4, 0x57471E) | While mode 13 is up a left press in the inventory window picks up nothing (only modes 8, 10, 11 and 4 pick, modes 1, 2 and 16 have their own branches), and the barter branch's close test exits for mode 13. | Original; Steamwoven's doll, grid and other buttons do nothing while the question is up |

The viewer passes the moved stack's own kind as the new stack's (`DropHeroAmmo`, a hero's new stack in `TakeAmmoFromNpc`) where the
original makes the type's base prototype (QW5); every installed stack is a base prototype, so nothing differs on installed data.

## Hero gold pile in the grid (ARC-UI-090)

Source: the executable (retail 1.0.7.4, read 2026-11-07, one reader; the steps of the gold transfer were read before, NT1 and AM7). Question: what
the hero's gold is in the original, because the drag of "the hero's gold" has no counter zone to take it from.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HG1 | gold getter 0x464780, pile finder 0x4647D0, transfer 0x464830, creation 0x464970, hit test 0x575CB0, pick 0x575FA0 | The PC's gold is not a counter: it is an item of type 8 (base prototype 9056, file 9076) in the PC's item list, named by critter field 0xE8, with its quantity in field 0xA5 (0x4647D0 reads field 0xE8 of a critter, 0x464780 the quantity of that item, 0 without one). The first transfer to a PC with no pile creates it (0x464970) and moves it in through the insertion (0x4617F0 → 0x466640), so it takes an inventory location like any item; a transfer of all its coins destroys it (0x43CCA0, which nulls field 0xE8). The window's hit test knows only the nine wear rectangles (locations 1000..1008), the other party's doll slots (2000..2009) and the grid cells; there is no coin zone, so a press on the pile is the grab of a grid item (IW2) and its release is a move (IW4, IW10), with the quantity question for a move to another holder or a drop (QW1). The header's gold figure (IW9) is a separate print of the same quantity. | Original (F1: the callers were followed, the pile prototype's flags were not read) |
| HG2 | move within the grid (0x5788C0, 0x466DA0, 0x466510) | The same-holder move of IW4, IW10–IW12 for an item of type 8: the target cells must be free of other items; the pile weighs nothing (AM1), so the load part of the insert check never refuses it. The remove check's no-drop flag and the item's scripts were not read for the gold prototype; this build assumes none (the pile's facts carry no flag and no script point). | Original (F2: inferred from the item moves for a type-8 item); Steamwoven moves the pile with `MoveHeroItem` |
| HG3 | order and size | Which of the pile and the starting kit enters the list first at a new game, and the pile's inventory art size, were not read. The build puts the pile after the kit and sizes it with the art source (prototype file 9076, cells by 0x461780), else 1 × 1. | Not read (PROJECT order); size Original through the art |
| HG4 | drop QW1/QW5/IW16, pickup 0x464830 | A release of the gold pile on the drop icon asks how many coins (QW1); the count is taken from the pile (the rest stays, an emptied pile is destroyed, HG1) and a new gold object with that count is placed on the hero's tile (a junk pile when items lie there, IW16); a pickup of that object transfers its coins back into the hero's pile (0x464830). | Original (F1, as HG1); Steamwoven: the session's `DropHeroGold` makes a gold item (`made:n`, coin record in the piles) on the ground and `TakePlaced` turns it back into the counter |

Steamwoven keeps the coins as the counter and the pile as a grid record `gold:pc` that appears with the first coin and goes with the last
([inventory runtime](../../world/inventory-runtime.md#hero-inventory-grid-arc-ui-019)). The drop, the quantity question and the throw of the pile
(QW1, QW5, IW18): since ARC-UI-091 the drop and the pickup back are built (HG4), since ARC-UI-094 the lift onto the game cursor and the throw too (the coins split off like a stack, QW5).

Button records hold screen coordinates; the creator finds the window under the point and subtracts its origin. An interface art number names its file through its `interface.mes` message, read at game start; the buttons ask for palette slot 0. | Original |

Top strip: Logbook (41, 2; 187), Character (4, 2; 169), Inventory (115, 2; 186), Map (78, 2; 193, its world-map art 194 swapped in by a game callback), Fate Points (157, 9; 137) and Sleep (605, 9; 137). Skills and Spells are made with flag 2 (a toggle), the rest with flag 1. The same init makes five hidden maintained-spell buttons on the top strip (art 188–192), the hot-key button (616, 455; art 182) and the magick weapon button (161, 443; art 563, bar mode 8). Game events light the Character, Logbook, Map and Inventory buttons through two callbacks (0x556A90 with the lit art at 0x5C6510, 0x556B70 for the map's town or world art), registered at 0x57C83E. | Original |
| GB3 | button library: create (0x537B50), mouse handler (to 0x5385BD), state set (0x537FE0); in-game filter press branch (0x54BD76) | A push button goes down on the press and acts on the release over it; a release elsewhere only lets it go, silently. While it is held no other button takes the pointer; it shows down over it and idle away from it. A toggle flips on each mouse press: it sends a press event turning on and a release event turning off, and the mouse release does nothing. When the pointer moves straight from one button to another, whether the first one goes back to idle is decided by the second one's toggle state (a display quirk, not modelled). Enter sets the hover message (`intgame.mes` 1000 Spells, 1001 Skills, 1002 Combat Toggle, 1003 Schematics, 1004 Log Book, 1005 Character Editor, 1006 Inventory, 1007 Maps, 1008 Fate Points, 1009 Sleep, 1010 Multiplayer Control); the next mouse move writes it in the message window; leaving clears it. | Original |

## Message log (ARC-UI-023)

The message log of the bottom strip's message window (bar mode 0 of 0x5506C0): what an entry is, which entries are kept, how the
window draws them, how the player scrolls, how the bar's button names reach it and how the spell and skill panes share the
window. Read on 2026-10-28 by this task and, independently, by a fresh agent that saw only the questions (method step 6).
The readers agree on every row; the second reader added that the repeat test ignores the case of ASCII letters (ML1), that a
pointer move above the strip gives the spells or skills pane's window to the log (ML8), that flag 1 of the text draw is never
tested and how the text draw measures and wraps (ML4), and the press-and-release event of the scroll areas (ML7). Every direct branch stays inside `.text`; the rows are F2. Coordinates are of the 800 × 600 screen (the
draw's window coordinates plus the bottom strip's origin 0, 441); art numbers are interface art numbers, message numbers those of
`mes\intgame.mes`.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ML1 | post (0x550750 → 0x552770), history globals (count 0x64C6C0, newest 0x64C6C4, shown 0x64C6C8, slots 0x64C540) | An entry holds a type, a text and two numbers. Types 0–5 and 13 are stored in a ring of ten slots: while it is not full the new entry takes the next slot, then the slot after the newest (the oldest goes); the text is copied with at most 199 characters. The new entry becomes the shown one and is drawn with the sound flag set. A type 4 or 5 entry whose text equals the newest stored entry's text (compared with the stored, cut copy by 0x58ADB0, the C
runtime's `_stricmp`: in the C locale A–Z fold to a–z, other bytes compare as they are) is not stored: the newest becomes the shown one and is drawn again with the sound flag. 0x550750 then calls an optional callback (0x64C6D4, set through 0x5507D0; not traced). | Original |
| ML2 | 0x552770 (0x52DFA0, 0x52DFB0, time 0x64C6EC); in-game filter mouse move (0x54B6CC) | Types 6–12 are never stored: such an entry is drawn only in the message mode and only when more than 3,000 ms of real time passed since the last stored entry (the stamp is taken at every stored post, repeats included); the shown position does not change. A pointer move while a bar button's hover message (0x64C674, GB5) is set posts it as a type 6 entry, so the name is written on entering and on every move over the button. | Original |
| ML3 | callers 0x45F8E3 (encumbrance, via 0x460630) and 0x4A6B84 (level up) | The encumbrance change posts a type 4 entry with `mes\critter.mes` 11 + level to the local PC; the level-up entry is type 0 (OL27). | Original |
| ML4 | draw (0x5529C0, types by table 0x5532E0), icon (0x554560), rectangles (0x5C70C8–0x5C7177), text draw (0x550A10, 0x51E190, 0x535570) | Each draw clears the text area and draws the type's icon at (217, 510). Type 0: title message 21 at (290, 504, 291 × 19), message 11 with the level at (290, 528, 291 × 18), then message 12 with the points at (290, 546, 291 × 18) or, when the points field is −1, the entry's text (the scheme text) at (290, 546, 291 × 36). Types 4, 5, 6 (and 12 without its fallback): a text with a line break draws the part before it as a title at (290, 504, 291 × 19) and the rest at (290, 528, 291 × 54); a text without one fills (290, 504, 291 × 78). The text draw (0x550A10) measures the text as one line, wraps it at the rectangle's width when it is wider, and draws it at the rectangle's top left (flags 0x2, 0x4, 0x8 and 0x10 would align it; the message window passes none, and flag 0x1 is never tested). When the last text does not fit its rectangle (the text library draws line after line while the height takes one and fails otherwise), types 4–6 clear the area and draw again without the icon at (217, 504, 364 × 19) and (217, 528, 364 × 54), or (217, 504, 364 × 78), with flag 0x20: characters are cut from the end until the text fits. | Original |
| ML5 | draw (0x5529C0), icon table (0x5C708C), sound (0x41B780) | Icons: type 0 art 438, 1 439, 2 440, 3 441, 4 442, 5 443, 6–13 444. With the sound flag set: type 0 plays 3005, 1 3006, 2 3003, 3 3002, 4 3004; types 5–13 play none. Types 2 and 3 take their body from 0x4C3D30 and 0x4C41E0 (posted at 0x4C3F49 and 0x4C43F9), 7–11 are hover descriptions of stats, skills, spells and tech (posted by the interface's own panes), 13 is stored and draws a title and a description from 0x56E9D0 (posted at 0x4B0274). | Original (types 1–3 and 9–13 not modelled; 7 and 8 by ARC-UI-035, HK20) |
| ML6 | font init (0x549BAA–0x549DEE, 0x5352E0), message mode background (0x5503F0, table 0x5C6548), clear (0x550930), text area (0x5C6D60, made window-relative at 0x54A592) | Titles use font 0x739F88 (font art 27, white); texts font 0x64C498 (font art 229, white); type 1's second line and the stat and tech hovers font 0x64C49C (font art 229, red). Building bar mode 0 draws art 354 at (196, 492); a clear fills the mode's text area (211, 503, 383 × 82) with black. | Original; built by ARC-DEV-156 (B27): the viewer draws the message window's texts and a pane's hover name in these two font arts with the MW31 blend, white tinted to the entry's colour; the wrap, the cut and the right edge as ML4, the pane name centred both ways as HK21, the line height the font's tallest glyph (PROJECT) |
| ML7 | scroll areas (records 0x5C65F8, 0x5C6608 made by 0x5501C0 → 0x54ABD0; shown by 0x551210 case 0), handlers (filter release 0x54BA85: 0x5528E0, 0x552930), redraw (0x552960), leave (0x54DC80 → 0x550720), mode build (0x551210) | Two invisible buttons with no art and no sound, 382 × 41: the upper one at (210, 504) shows the next older entry (0x5528E0, at most as far back as the count), the lower one at (210, 545) the next newer one (0x552930, up to the newest); both act on the button library's release over the pressed button (button event 1, sub-event 1) in the message mode, unless an item is held (0x573620; then the held item is dropped through 0x575770). The redraw (0x552960) first switches the window to the message mode (0x5506C0(0)), then draws the shown entry without the sound flag. Leaving a bar button clears the hover message and fills the text area with black (0x550720), and building the message mode draws only its background: the window stays blank until the next entry or scroll click. | Original; the blank window is a technical display bug: Steamwoven draws the shown entry again after a hover and on the return to the message mode, in both rule modes (owner rule 2026-10-28) |
| ML8 | in-game filter mouse move (0x54B70B–0x54B79A), mode set (0x5506C0, 0x552130) | A new stored entry puts the window in the message mode even while Spells or Skills is down (ML7's redraw). Then a pointer move inside x 10–790, y 441–590 in the message mode switches back to spells (mode 1) when the Spells toggle is down (button state 0), else to skills (mode 2) when Skills is down. A pointer move above the strip (y below 441) in mode 1 or 2 switches to the message mode (0x54B7C0), so the spells and skills panes own the window only while the pointer is over the bottom strip; the weapon's spells (mode 8) are not switched. 0x5506C0 with the current mode goes to mode 0 (a toggle). | Original |

## Window key modifiers (ARC-INPUT-004)

Source: the [WORLD keyboard route](../../input/diagnostic-palette.md) and
[KM-07](../../requirements/original-keyboard-mouse.md#km-07--переносимость-key-identity-и-модификаторов). Question: do the
original's window letters (L, C, I, T, W, R and the other letters of the character table) act while Ctrl, Shift or Alt is
held? Read on 2026-10-27 by this task and, independently, by a fresh agent that saw only the questions (method step 6);
both agree on every row. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`; the indirect ones (the window procedure's message
switch, the filter's character and scancode tables) stay inside their functions. The rows are F2.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WK1 | message pump (0x52B7B0), window procedure (0x52B9D0), enqueue (0x52B890) | Every message goes through `TranslateMessage` (the game sets no pre-translate hook). WM_CHAR is the only keyboard message the window procedure turns into a game message: a character message (type 4) carrying the low byte of `wParam`. WM_KEYDOWN, WM_KEYUP, WM_SYSKEYDOWN, WM_SYSKEYUP and WM_SYSCHAR go to `DefWindowProcA`. Of the enqueue's 19 callers only the WM_CHAR case writes type 4. | Original |
| WK2 | in-game filter (0x54B5D0, a copy at 0x54C8E0), 0x57DC60 | The character branch acts on the character alone, with no modifier read: space, C, F, I, L, R, S, T and W in both cases through one table (character − 0x20, at most 0x57), A and a in 0x57DC60. A code below 0x20 (Ctrl+letter's control code) fails the range test and does nothing, except 0x0D, handled first as Enter; codes from 0x78 and bytes from 0x80 are ignored too. | Original |
| WK3 | in-game filter scancode tables (0x54C3E8, 0x54C57D), keyboard poll (0x52B380) | No scancode branch acts on A, C, F, I, L, R, S, T or W (key down: Backspace, Delete, `,` `.` `/`, the numpad block; key up: K, M, `,` `.` `/`). The poll queues no scancode message of a non-modifier key while either Ctrl or either Alt is held (Shift is not tested). | Original (the poll's Ctrl/Alt drop is not modelled: the viewer keeps its other keys' modifier rules) |
| WK4 | logbook open (0x53F0E0) | With left Ctrl and left Alt held the logbook opens in an alternate state (sound 0xBBA), cleared on close; this path is reached from the logbook button, since Ctrl+Alt+L makes no character on a standard layout. | Original (not modelled) |

What Windows does, not the executable: Ctrl+letter is a control code (Ctrl+M is 0x0D, Enter), Alt+letter is WM_SYSCHAR,
AltGr and the active layout decide the character (a Cyrillic layout gives bytes from 0x80, so the letters act only on a
Latin layout). So in the original, L and Shift+L (or Caps Lock) open the logbook, and Ctrl+L, Ctrl+Shift+L, Alt+L and
Ctrl+Alt+L do nothing; the same holds for A, C, F, I, R, S, T and W. Steamwoven (`OriginalWorldKeyboard.CharacterKeySilenced`)
silences these letters under Ctrl, Alt or Command (Command is the Mac system's), lets Shift through, and an open window's
own letter does not close it under those modifiers either. Not located in this pass: where O (options), V (version) and E
(the turn-based sub-turn) are read; they stay reserved keys at F0 with their earlier addresses.

## Combat HUD (ARC-UI-017)

Source: alpha-gap item N4 (FR-COMBAT-011, FR-UI-002, FR-UI-005) and the [combat HUD study](../combat-hud.md): what the
original shows of a fight on screen, the turn-based action points and whose turn it is, the health and fatigue of the PC,
of a hovered critter and of the followers, and the floating text over objects. Read on 2026-10-28 by this task and,
independently, by a fresh agent that saw only the questions (method step 6). The readers agree on every row; the second
reader added that the pending cost stays set after a charge (CH4), the striped bar and its outline (CH3), that the
indicator module is an unpresented load indicator (CH6), the number formats (CH7), that the low-health mark is a red fill
over the portrait, not an outline (CH9, checked again by the first reader: 0x51E0A0 fills through 0x523120), and that
stacked lines stay invisible until they reach the start offset (CH11). Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. Art
numbers are the interface art list's; message lines are those of `mes\combat.mes` (by number only). Colours are RGB: the
colour helpers combine three per-channel tables, 0x739F2C red, 0x739F28 green and 0x739E98 blue (their masks come from the
display's pixel format, 0x5248D7 → 0x52C070). The rows are F2.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CH1 | combat interface init (0x56EA90), build (0x56ED20), show (0x56EC00), close (0x56EF40) | Init loads the lamp arts 295, 296 and 315 (table 0x5CAA98) and registers five callbacks with the combat module (0x4B6C40, slots 0x5FC1E0–0x5FC1F0): 0x56ECE0 (combat mode change), 0x56ED00 (build, at a round's start and on loading a save in a round), 0x56EF40 (close, at a round's end), 0x56F430 (action points, CH2) and 0x56F990 (CH6). The build makes two windows: the lamp panel (art 294 at 222, 412 of the 800 × 600 screen, window 0x5CAA18) with the end-turn button (art 297 at 320, 9 of that panel; its id in 0x5CAA44, CT5), and a 124 × 14 bar at 617, 577 (window 0x5CAA1C). Only one is shown: the lamp panel while the main interface bar is shown, the bar while it is hidden (0x5578C0); the resize handler applies the same rule. The close destroys both. | Original |
| CH2 | action-point display (0x56F430), lamp positions (0x5CAA48) | Called with a number of points A; the pending cost P is the last action-point check's (0x4B7C20, global 0x5FC244). It redraws the panel and then lights lamps from the left (four groups of five, 13 px apart): first A − P lamps of art 295 (the points left after the action), then min(A, P) of art 296 (the action's cost), then P − A of art 315 (the cost the points do not cover). At most 20 lamps: with A ≥ P the points left come first (all 20 of art 295 when A − P ≥ 20) and the cost fills what remains of the 20; with A < P the cost's min(A, 20) lamps come first and the shortfall fills what remains. A = 0 lights nothing. | Original |
| CH3 | bar display (0x56EFA0, 0x56F2F0) | The same three shares on the hidden-interface bar, on a 25-point scale (120 px, 4.8 px a point, capped at 25, from x 2): green (0, 255, 0) for the points left, orange (255, 128, 0) for the cost and red (255, 0, 0) for the shortfall, each a striped band (the colour on odd columns, black on even ones), on black inside a 1 px blue (0, 0, 255) outline. Drawn only on the local PC's subturn (0x4B6D80, 0x40D9F0). | Original |
| CH4 | callers of slot 0x5FC1EC: subturn start (0x4B6FAA, 0x4B6FC5), action-point check (0x4B77E9), charge (0x4B7D72, 0x4B7DAA), subturn end (0x4B7660), save load (0x4B21EE) | A subturn start gives the participant its points (stat 11, at least 5) and shows them when the participant is the local PC, otherwise 0 (no lamps). Every action-point check of the current participant, the cost preview the cursor makes for an action, stores its cost and, for the local PC, redraws its points, so the lamps show the hovered action's cost. A covered charge redraws the points left; the PC's overexertion (CT4) shows 0; the end of the local PC's subturn shows 0. The pending cost is not cleared by a charge (only a load resets it), so after paying the lamps preview the same action again until the next check. | Original |
| CH5 | other participant's cue (0x56F660, 0x56F840), interface timer (0x57D370, event 12 handled at 0x57D59F) | While the panel exists the cue re-arms itself every 100 ms. On a subturn that is not the local PC's it redraws the panel with three lamps of art 295 at positions i, i − 7 and i + 7 (modulo 20) and advances i by one, so three lights run round the lamps once every 2 s; the hidden-interface bar shows a 4 px striped green block moving along it instead. On the PC's own subturn it draws nothing. The build starts i at 0. | Original |
| CH6 | slot 0x5FC1F0 callback (0x56F990), indicator module (0x4BB720–0x4BB980) | No turn order: the combat interface calls no text routine and draws no participant list, name or order. 0x56F990 only refreshes the local PC handle (0x40DA50). The module next to it is a load indicator (art 601) whose buffer nothing presents; its counter of the PC's subturns (0x4BB8E0, 0x4BB8F0, 0x4BB900) is read only by the subturn start and its per-subturn hook (0x4BB910) is empty. The combat interface shows only "the PC's points" or "someone else's turn" (CH5). | Original; Steamwoven adds a list of the round's participants with the current one marked, shown in the Steamwoven rule mode and hidden in Original (owner decision 2026-10-28, [decisions](../../architecture/decisions.md)) |
| CH7 | PC gauges (0x54AF10, numbers 0x54AD00) | Two vertical gauges in the interface bar (bar origin 0, 441): health at 14, 472 and fatigue at 754, 473, each 28 × 88. The fill rises from the bottom over current / maximum of the 88 px (half when the maximum is 0) in art 18 (health), 17 (health while poisoned, stat 24 above 0) or 19 (fatigue), its top edge drawn 8 px higher; the empty part is art 20 over the background art 184. The current health and fatigue are printed as three zero-padded digits under the gauges, and a poisoned PC's poison level beside the health gauge. Read again in ARC-UI-020: the percent is current × 100 / maximum by signed integer division (truncated toward zero), the fill is height × percent / 100 truncated the same way, and the empty part is drawn only when its height is above 0; the numbers go through 0x54AD00 with the format `%0*d` at three digits (C's `%03d`, so a negative value keeps its sign within the width): health slot 0 at 15, 578, fatigue slot 1 at 755, 578 and poison slot 5 at 15, 500 (slot rectangles 0x5C63F8, so the poison level is printed over the health gauge), the poison only when above 0. Read again in ARC-COMBAT-024: the health number is the current hit points of 0x43D600 (maximum hit points 0x43D5A0 minus the damage field 0x1D, 0x43D510), passed to 0x54AD00 with no clamp, so a dead PC (damage 32000, the death's pinned value) prints a large negative number (maximum 48: "-31952") until the death screen; the viewer prints the same. | Original |
| CH8 | hovered-object panel (0x553F70), bar helper (0x554640), line rects (0x5C70C8–0x5C70F8) | For a critter under the cursor the bar shows its name, a level line, a health bar (arts 463 full and 464 empty, 665 and 666 while poisoned) and a fatigue bar (465, 466), left to right, 291 px wide. The percent is current × 100 / maximum (no guard for a zero maximum), clamped to 0–100 for the bar; the filled width is width × p / 100 rounded down, plus one unless that is the whole width. The text is "current/maximum" when the critter is the observing PC or one of its followers (PC leader, 0x45DDA0), otherwise "p%". Read again in ARC-UI-020: the text formats are `%d/%d` and `%d%%` (0x5C7848, 0x5C7850) and the percent text is the unclamped one (a dead critter's can be negative); the bar helper draws no filled part at 0 %; the zero-maximum division is a technical bug (a crash), which Steamwoven reads as 0 % in both rule modes. | Original |
| CH9 | follower portraits (0x56B290, art helper 0x56B510, slots 0x5CA360) | Up to six portraits on the screen's left edge (40 × 49 at 11, 50 + 62 × slot, scrolled through the follower list): frame art 503, the portrait at 4, 4, a health bar (art 505, 616 while poisoned) at 3, 39 and a fatigue bar (art 504) at 3, 44, each cropped to the follower's current / maximum percent. Below 20 % health the portrait's 32 × 32 area gets a red (255, 0, 0) fill added over it (0x51E0A0), so the face turns red. Read again in ARC-UI-020: each percent is current × 100 / maximum by signed integer division with no zero guard (the same technical bug as CH8, read as 0 % in Steamwoven); the bar helper 0x56B510 draws nothing at a percent of 0 or less, caps it at 100 and crops the art to width × percent / 100 rounded down; the tint test is the truncated health percent below 20 (`cmp 0x14`), so a dead follower is red too. Read again in ARC-DEV-360 (the fatigue bar, exe 0x56B290 at 0x56B44F–0x56B480): the fatigue percent is the current fatigue 0x45D700 (maximum fatigue 0x45D670 minus the fatigue damage field 0xE2 read by 0x45D550, no clamp) × 100 / the maximum, drawn with art 504 at 3, 44 (the health bar before it, art 505 or 616, at 3, 39, both through 0x56B510 with its last argument 100); there is no magick-point bar. The fatigue-damage setter 0x45D5A0 (also called by the recovery event 0x45E610) writes the field, clamps it at 0 and calls the interface hook in slot 0x14 of the callback table (0x45FD40 → 0x5E866C, the builder 0x57C6E0 stores 0x57CC10 there); 0x57CC10 → 0x56B4D0 draws the frame again (0x56B290) when the follower frames exist (flag 0x67BC60) and the critter's PC leader (0x45DDA0) is the PC, so a fatigue write or a recovery changes the bar at once. The viewer's bar did the same (values from the life rules, the original's art 504 cropped from its left, redrawn on the next frame after any session revision); the `life` smoke now checks the fatigue bar shrinking with a fatigue packet and rising with the follower's 80-second recovery event. | Original |
| CH10 | floater init (0x4D4E20), settings (0x4D5B10, 0x4D5B40, 0x4D5B60, getter 0x4D5180) | Five floater fonts (font art 229), by index: 0 white (255, 255, 255), 1 red (255, 0, 0), 2 green (0, 255, 0), 3 blue (64, 64, 255), 4 yellow (255, 255, 0). Two settings (OP5): "text floaters" 0–2, default 1, and "float speed", default 2, read as a step of speed + 1 px. Level 0 draws no floater (the draw, 0x4D5310, also skips in a view mode set through 0x4D5130); a line is made at any level unless its caller checks the level (CH12). | Original; ARC-DEV-156 (B28): the viewer draws the lines in font art 229 with the MW31 blend, each in its colour and with the fade as the blit's opacity; the line height h is the font's tallest glyph + 2 (PROJECT: the height getter 0x4D4E20 reads is not read) |
| CH11 | floater create (0x4D5450), tick (0x4D5190), place (0x4D58C0, 0x4D5A60), fade (0x4D5AA0) | Each object keeps a list of lines (h = the font height + 2). A new line starts 4h below the top of the object's box, or, when the object's newest line has risen less than h, one h below that line, so lines stack instead of overlapping. At most every 50 ms every line rises by the step (60 px a second at the default) and is removed once past the top; an object with no line left is dropped. The box is 200 px wide and 5h high, centred over the object (x = its screen point + 40 − 100, bottom 70 px above the point, both plus the object's draw offset) and moves with it; each line is centred in it. Opacity, with m = the line's offset + h / 2 and m above 3h counted as 5h − m: opaque above 2h, 255 × (m − h / 2) / 1.5h below that, 0 at h / 2 or less: a line fades in from 4h to 2.5h, is opaque to 1.5h and fades out to the top; a line stacked below 4h stays invisible until it reaches 4h. Read again in ARC-UI-021: the creation makes the line whatever the level; the tick steps when the time since its last step is 50 ms or more (one step however long the gap) and removes a line whose offset falls below 0 (one at 0 stays); the fade is m = offset + h / 2 (h / 2 truncated), m above 3h → 5h − m, 255 above 2h, else 255 × (m − h / 2) / (2h − h / 2) through the x87 (single float 255.0, truncated by `_ftol`) when m > h / 2, else 0; the box's left is the screen x + draw offset + 40 − 100 and its top the screen y + draw offset + 20 − 5h − 90, a line's left the box's + (200 − width) / 2 truncated. | Original |
| CH12 | damage (0x4B4390), attack (0x4B3170 at 0x4B3636), blinding (0x4B81B0), hard-target damage (0x4B5580) | The damaged critter's colour is red for a PC, yellow for an NPC whose PC leader is the local PC and white otherwise. At level 2 only: the damage line in that colour, "N line-1" for the health damage followed by " N line-10" for fatigue damage; with fatigue damage and no health damage (0 or less) only the fatigue part, and with neither the health part even for 0; and a poison increase "line-0 +N" in green. At every level, in that colour over the critter: the critical hit (12) and the damage multipliers (13–15), and the critical effects by the damage flags (lines 3–9, 16–24); the critical miss (2) over the attacker. A dodge (11) floats in white over the target, blinding (4) in red for a PC and white otherwise. A non-critter object's damage is "N line-1" in white at level 2. An attacker hurt by striking a hard target floats in red over itself: the damage line at level 2 without a weapon, the weapon's damage or break message (8, 23) at any level with one. The healing function (0x4B58C0) creates no floater. Read again in ARC-UI-021 (the order and the texts): an object's damage line is "line-1 N" (`%s %d`, the damage after the portal and container reduction, never below 0), not "N line-1". For a critter, in this order and all in the critter's colour: the critical miss (context flags 0x18) or the critical hit (0x4); the multiplier ×3, ×2 or ×1.5 (damage flags 0x20, 0x10, 0x8); the stun (16) whenever the stun bit meets a critter not stunned without a knockdown or knockout bit, also when the stun animation then cannot start; with a knockdown or knockout bit, unconsciousness (17) for a knockout on a critter not already unconscious (none when it is), else the knockdown (18); the scar (3) on the scar bit, whatever the scar effect did; the blinding (4) when not blinded; the arm (5) when an arm was crippled now, then on any arm bit the weapon (19) and the shield slot's item (20) each dropped when its wear check refuses it; the legs (6) when not crippled; the armour (24 broken, else 7); the helmet's drop (20); the weapon's damage (23 broken, else 8); its destruction (22), else the ammunition (9, whether any was lost) and the weapon's drop (19). A drop's message floats whatever the drop then does. The damage line is `%d %s` (health, always, even 0 or negative), then ` %d %s` (fatigue, with its leading space) appended when the health part is above 0 and written alone otherwise; poison `%s %+d`. A target dead before the step floats nothing. The weapon wear's messages over the attacker are in red (font 1) at any level; the bare-handed loss is `%d %s` with line 1 at level 2. | Original |
| CH13 | damage (0x4B4390) callers, kill notification (0x45DA20), dying start (0x42D570) (ARC-DEV-296) | Re-read for the callers that are no weapon swing or shot. The damage function prints its floaters inside the step with no test of who called it (the floater level and the damage packet are all it reads), and it plays no sound of its own (its other call targets for a hit are the multiplayer description helper, taken only with the network flag, and the victim and source history writer 0x4F5690, which only stores a record). So a spell's, a trap's, a burn's and a script's damage on a critter float the CH12 lines, and no weapon hit sound plays for them (those belong to the attack functions, SN27). The kill notification plays no sound either: the dying bark comes from the dying animation's start (SN26), so every NPC death that plays its dying animation barks, whoever dealt it; a death of an NPC lying in its fall is not played (0x45D730) and so is silent. | Original |

## Panel art (ARC-UI-024)

Source: FR-UI-002, the in-game panel's art after the interface bars (GB1–GB7) and the life bars (CH7–CH9): how the PC's
gauges draw their vials, the compact panel the original shows instead of the bars, the follower frames' images and the
hovered critter's portrait. Read on 2026-10-28 by this task. Art names are `art\interface\interface.mes`'s; an interface
art id is built by 0x504330 (number, frame, a flag bit, palette 0–3), so a literal frame 0 is the art's first frame.
Colours are RGB through the per-channel tables of the Combat HUD section. The rows are F1.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PA1 | PC gauges (0x54AF10), art id builder (0x504330) | The vial arts are 17 `yelovial` (1 frame), 18 `redvial` and 19 `bluvial` (15 frames each), all 28 × 96, and 20 `clrvial` (1 frame, 34 × 90). The gauge asks for frame 0 of each with palette 0; no other caller of the id builder passes these numbers as literals, so the 14 other frames of 18 and 19 were not found drawn. | Original |
| PA2 | PC gauges (0x54AF10, clip 0x52D1A0, blit 0x51DA80) | For each gauge the gauge rectangle is first redrawn from the bottom strip's art 184 (its own place in the strip). With fill = height × percent / 100 (CH7) and empty = 88 − fill: when empty is above 0 the empty vial art 20 is drawn over the gauge's top `empty` rows, cut from the art's top left (its first 28 columns). | Original |
| PA3 | PC gauges (0x54AF10) | When fill is above 0 the fill art (18, 17 or 19, CH7) is placed with its top at the gauge's top + empty − 8 and drawn down to the gauge's bottom: fill + 8 rows from its first row, so its top 8 rows (the liquid's surface) overlap the empty part; when that would start above the gauge (fill above 80) the drawing starts at the gauge's top with the art's row 8 − empty, so a full gauge shows the art's rows 8 to 95. The art's rows below fill + 8 are not drawn. A fill above 88 (current above the maximum) draws from a lower art row, and a negative one (a dead PC) skips the fill art and draws the empty art over more than 88 rows (the art has 90): technical drawing overflows that Steamwoven clamps to the gauge in both rule modes. | Original; overflows clamped (technical) |
| PA4 | interface hide toggle (0x5578E0, allowed by 0x5578D0), compact UI create (0x568A40), destroy (0x568C90), component table (0x5CA198–0x5CA224), refresh (0x568CF0, called at the end of 0x54AF10) | The interface can be hidden only when the game was started with `-fullscreen` (0x4011F5 → 0x5578D0, and the start toggles it once): the toggle hides both strip windows, widens the world view and creates the compact UI; showing the bars destroys it. The compact UI is a container window at (210, 479, 385, 84) and two components: "Health/Fatigue Bar", a window at (751, 498), 49 × 102, with colour key (0, 155, 0), drawn by 0x569070; and "Hotkey Bar" (25, 25, 25, 25), whose init, message and draw functions do nothing (0x569570–0x5695A0). The PC gauges' redraw also redraws the components while the compact UI exists. | Original; the viewer shows its compact panel while its bars are not drawn |
| PA5 | compact life panel (0x569070) | The window is filled with its colour key (transparent), outlined in (9, 9, 9) over 0, 0, 49, 102, with a (9, 9, 9) line from (24, 1) to (24, 100). Health column x 1, width 23: d = hit point damage × 100 / maximum (signed, truncated, no zero guard: a division by zero for a zero maximum); the fill starts at row min(d + 1, 100) and has 100 − that rows, one more while d is below 100 (nothing at d ≥ 100); red (255, 0, 0), green (0, 255, 0) while poisoned (stat 24 above 0). Fatigue column x 25, width 23, the same from the fatigue damage, the start capped at 101, blue (0, 0, 255). Each number is the current value printed `%02d` in font 229 (`Flare12Font`) white (font table 0x64C170 entry 0, colour (255, 255, 255)), centred in its 23-pixel column at its fill's start row (also when nothing is filled); a poisoned PC's poison level `%02d` 40 rows lower in the health column. The poison level is drawn first and leaves its centred x in the shared rectangle (0x5692CE), to which the health number adds its own centring offset (0x5693BA–0x5693D0): a poisoned PC's health number stands right of centre by the poison text's offset. | Original; zero maximum read as fully damaged, and the health number centred while poisoned (both technical: a division by zero and a reused rectangle) |
| PA6 | hover image (0x553D10), hovered panel draw (0x553F70 → 0x5561D0 at 217, 69 of the bottom strip), follower frame (0x56B290), portrait draws (0x4CE470 at the image's size, 0x4CE680 at 32 × 32), bar helper (0x56B510) | The message window's hovered-object panel draws the object's image at (217, 510) of the screen (217, 69 of the bottom strip; 211, 59 less in the compact UI). The image is the portrait (0x4CEB80, field 0xE7) for a PC, and for an NPC whose PC leader is the observer when its portrait is not 0; otherwise an interface art by type (portals, containers, scenery, items by their own rules, a critter by its flags or race, table 0x5C6FA0). A portrait drawn at its own size uses the small `.bmp` (size 0 below 128). The original has no fixed PC portrait or name area in the bars: the PC's portrait shows only there while the PC is hovered. A follower frame draws the same choice at 4, 4: the portrait at 32 × 32, or the art through 0x56B510 at 50 % scale (the helper's arguments are the art, x, y, the cropped percent and the scale percent). | Original |

"the original has no sort buttons"). Read on 2026-11-10 by this task in the open 0x572815..0x57287C and 0x57341F, the mouse handler 0x573850
(0x573A8D..0x573B36), the arrange function 0x4670A0 with its two free-slot finders 0x4675A0 and 0x4676A0, the grid builder 0x466260, the item size
0x461780, the button creator 0x537B50 and the field setter 0x4EFDD0 (callees 0x4EDD50, 0x4601F0 and 0x4EDE80 only named). The rows are F1 (one reader). The
arrange button is the button 340 `tiny_butt` that [IW30](#loot-window-art-arc-ui-052) lists without a purpose.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| IW37 | open 0x572815..0x57287C, creator 0x537B50, art id 0x504330 | Every mode of the window (0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6) makes the button first, before the mode's other buttons: interface art 340 `tiny_butt` (two frames of 28 × 27 pixels) at (763, 197) of the window (the creator adds the window's origin; the doll toggles at (713, 37), (751, 37) and the counts at x 729 use the same frame of reference), its handle in 0x681444. Its descriptor gives the press sound 3000, the release sound 3001 and no sound for the pointer entering or leaving, as every art button of the bars does ([GB6](#interface-bars-arc-ui-018)). The art shows scattered items, a red arrow and an ordered grid (the owner's screenshot). | Original; built by ARC-DEV-339 in the own, loot, steal and barter windows (drawn with its first frame as the other art buttons are; its pressed frame is not drawn) |
| IW38 | handler 0x573850, button release 0x573A8D..0x573B36, open tail 0x57341F | A button event of subtype 1 (the release, after a press on the button; the button system decides that) whose handle is 0x681444 calls the arrange function 0x4670A0 for the player (0x6814F8) with the flag 0x6814F0, then 0x466260 to build the player's grid again (buffer 0x68111C); when 0x6810FC is set it does the same for the shown object (0x6813A8, buffer 0x681518). 0x6810FC is set only in the barter mode (mode 1) when a getter of the other party (0x45DE00 on 0x682C78, not read; probably its leader) answers null, so for a shop's NPC and not for a follower; every other mode clears it. Then the flag becomes 1 − flag whatever the arrangement did, and the window is redrawn (0x576520 with 0). The open clears the flag at its end (0x57341F), so the presses alternate: the first after the open uses the horizontal layout (flag 0), the next the vertical one (flag 1), and so on. A press with an item on the cursor is not served by the handler's own press branch; the button system is not read for that case. | Original; built by ARC-DEV-339 for the player's grid (a held item makes the press do nothing); PROJECT: the shop's NPC is not arranged (the viewer's wares have no grid slots; its 0x6810FC reading is F0; in the original the shown object may be a container substitute with 96 rows) |
| IW39 | arrange 0x4670A0, finders 0x4675A0 (flag 0) and 0x4676A0 (flag other than 0), size 0x461780, setter 0x4EFDD0 | A network game whose flag bit 0 is set and bit 1 clear (a client) sends the request to the server (0x4EDE80) and does nothing else. Otherwise: the holder's list is the critter's (fields 0xED, 0xEE) unless its type (field 0x14F) is 2 (a container: 0x3B, 0x3C); an empty list ends the function. The list is copied into a handle array. Every item whose inventory location (field 0x5E, 0x461340) is 1000..1008 (worn) or 2000..2009 (quick slot) is **left out**: it is swapped with the array's last item, the count shrinks and the swapped-in item is examined next (so the order of the rest changes). The rest is sorted by an exchange sort: position i takes any later item that is taller, or, at equal height, wider (ties never move), so items come tallest first, then widest first, with ties in a scrambled list order. A zeroed grid (96 × 10 cells) is then filled item by item in that order: flag 0 asks 0x4675A0 (rows from the top, columns from the left within a row), any other flag 0x4676A0 (columns from the left, rows from the top within a column), the first top-left cell whose whole rectangle is free, and marks the item's rectangle (0x466310). The size is the inventory art's frame in 32-pixel cells (0x461780, JP3). **When an item finds no place the function stops and writes nothing at all**; otherwise every item's location is written (0x4EFDD0 on field 0x5E; a network host also sends the field) and the holder is told it changed (0x4EDD50 → 0x4601F0). A critter's grid has 12 rows. | Original; built by ARC-DEV-339 (`InventoryArrangeRules`, 0x4670A0 partial: the client branch is not built; 0x4676A0 full); `GameSessionOwner.ArrangeHeroGrid` for the hero; PROJECT: the list order is the session's (kit, taken, made, then the gold pile, whose place in the original's list was not read) |
| IW40 | arrange 0x4670A0 (no callee below), IW8, IW12, IW15 | The arrangement writes the locations directly: **no insert or remove check** (0x466510, 0x466DA0: so no weight test, no Technophobia test and no refusal), **no item script event** (the move's remove-item, wield, insert-item events of IW15 come from 0x441980, which the function never calls), **no action points** (IW8) and no message or sound beyond the button's own. An item that cannot be moved by hand (the no-drop flag) is arranged too, and the item on the cursor (item flag 0x10) is just another list item. The arrangement therefore changes no weight, no encumbrance and no hotkey entry. | Original; built by ARC-DEV-339 (one session revision, no events, no checks) |

world's mouse move 0x551910, the hovered-object setter 0x43C270, its draws 0x43C5C0 and 0x43C690, the description dispatch 0x553BE0,
the windows 0x555D80, 0x555EC0, 0x556040 and 0x553F70, the image choice 0x553D10 and the screen pick 0x43D9F0. Art names are
`art\interface\interface.mes`'s. Colours are RGB through the per-channel tables of the Combat HUD section. The rows are F1 (one reader).

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OH1 | world mouse move 0x551910, pick 0x4F2CB0, screen pick 0x43D9F0 | The pointer's object is found by the spell UI's pick with the default mask (0x4F2CB0, SU16); a found object is described for the local PC (0x57CCF0) and made the hovered object (0x43C270). The screen pick skips an object whose flags (field 0x13) hold 0x800 (`OF_CLICK_THROUGH`) unless the editor global 0x5E2F58 is 1, and a type the table 0x5E2ED4 switches off (every type is on in the game, 0x43A41E); the wrapper refuses a click-through object too (SU17). So a click-through object is never hovered, highlighted or described. | Original |
| OH2 | hovered-object setter 0x43C270 (its clear 0x43C4A0, getter 0x43C570) | The same handle does nothing. Otherwise the old object's rectangle is redrawn, the handle stored, the highlight colour set to white (255, 255, 255) and the rings to arts 467 `hoverunder` and 468 `hoverover`; no object: nothing more. A wall (type 0) or projectile (4) gets no highlight. A portal, container, scenery or item (types 1–3, 5–14) is only marked. A critter or trap (15–17) gets the rings (flag 0x5E2E94) when there is a local PC: an NPC's colour is its reaction category's (0x4C0CC0 → 0x4C0FC0, table 0x5E2E78; R15), and while the local PC is in combat mode (0x4B3D50) both rings are art 555 `hoverattack`. The getter drops a handle whose object is gone. | Original (the rings not built) |

It is skipped for an object with flag 0x100 or 0x200 (`OF_DONTDRAW`, `OF_INVISIBLE`) unless the player detects the invisible (0x458A80 with the mask 0x200, PT7). A wading object is drawn in two parts (its lowest 15 rows, then the rest), both with the same blit; a shrunk object's rectangle is doubled. Steamwoven reads the constant colour as a tint of the source pixel before the add, as for the light gradients (0x20000, four corner colours), so the object brightens by about 78 % of its own colour (the pixel loop of the 16-bit blitter not read). | Original |
| OH4 | ring draws 0x43C5C0 (under) and 0x43C690 (over) | A critter's ring art 467 (or 555) is drawn under the objects with the order 0x5F5E0FF and art 468 (or 555) over them, both additive in the highlight colour, animated at the art's own rate (0x5E2EC4, 1000 / fps ms). | Original (not built) |

Further lines on the fourth line (0x5C70F8: 290, 564): a portal's and a container's lock state (`intgame.mes` 26 or 27 by their flags, fields 0x2E and 0x38, bits 0x2 and 0x4, else 9 or 10 by 0x441D40) and right-aligned the hit points "%d/%d" (0x43D600, 0x43D5A0; a container's and scenery's only without object flag 0x400000); then a call for scenery flag 0x8 (field 0x45). | Original (image, name and, since ARC-UI-107, the fourth line built: OH10) |

| OH10 | the fourth line of 0x555EC0, 0x556040, 0x555D80; text draw 0x550A10; lock 0x441D40 with its gate 0x441CF0 (prototype read 0x49B290); hit points 0x43D600 over 0x43D5A0; format 0x5C7848 | Read on 2026-11-08 by ARC-UI-107 (B37, the same executable as above, F1: one reader; the PR's independent review re-read 0x555D80, 0x555EC0, 0x556040, 0x550A10, 0x43D5A0, 0x43D600, 0x441D40 and the rectangles and found them as written here). **Rectangle and font.** The line is the rectangle 0x5C70F8, 290 × 18 at the window's (290, 123), which is (290, 564) on the 800 × 600 screen; the lock text and the hit-point text are both drawn through 0x550A10 with the font 0x64C49C, the text font (font art 229) the ML6 row gives as red: a fixed colour, not a function of the hit-point ratio or the palette. 0x550A10 takes the window, the rectangle, the text, the font and a flag word: flag 1 starts the text at the rectangle's left edge; flag 2 right-aligns it (the start is the left edge plus the width less the text's width, when the text is narrower than the rectangle); 4 centres it, 8 centres vertically, 0x10 anchors at the middle, 0x20 lets a too-wide text be cut. **Hit points.** The text is `%d/%d` (0x5C7848) with the current value first: current (0x43D600) is the maximum less the damage (0x43D510), maximum (0x43D5A0) the hit-point points plus the adjustment for these objects (a critter adds more, DU1–DU2): no clamp, so the current value may be 0 or negative. The draw functions read the values when they run; that nothing redraws the window while the pointer stays is inferred from the callers of 0x553BE0 (hover entry, not traced). **Per type.** A portal draws its lock line, then its hit points always (0x555FE3 tests no object flag). A container draws its lock line, then its hit points only when object flag 0x400000 (invulnerable; field 0x13) is clear (0x55616E). Scenery has no lock line and draws the hit points under the same flag test (0x555E43), then the town-map call of WM127. **Lock line.** The portal reads its lock flags (field 0x2E), the container its own (field 0x38); bit 0x2 (jammed) gives `intgame.mes` 26, else bit 0x4 (magically held) gives 27, else 0x441D40 decides: non-zero gives 9, zero 10. The text is drawn left-aligned (flag 1). 0x441D40 answers 0 for an object that is not lockable (0x441CF0: a portal or container whose prototype number, less 20, is not 0xBCF: the junk pile is not), for lock bits 0x80 and 0x8, for bit 0x20 outside the game hours 7–21 and for bit 0x40 inside them, else it returns bit 0x1 (LK1–LK2). | Original; built by ARC-UI-107 (`AreaHoverWindow.Window`, `Condition`, `LockMessage`, `HitPointText`; the draw `AreaMessageLayout.Draw`); the line is read once when the pointer enters the object, as the original draws it |
| OH11 | critter window 0x553F70 (second line, dead line, alignment image), rectangle 0x5C70D8, stat bounds 0x4B0FC0 and 0x4B0FD0, categories 0x4C0FC0 and 0x4C1010, sight 0x4AF260, hearing 0x4AF470, sleeping test 0x45D870, concealed test 0x45EDE0, dead test 0x45D8D0 | Read on 2026-11-10 by ARC-UI-109 (F1: one reader, the retail 1.0.7.4 executable, SHA-256 checked; the PR's independent review re-read it). **Arguments:** the observing PC and the described object (two handles each) and the name text (the window is the message window's own). **Order.** The object's type (field 0x14F) and the PC's spell flags (field 0x14) bit 0x80 (detecting alignment) are read; for that case the object's alignment stat (19) too. When the object is another critter than the PC and the PC is in combat mode and the critter is not dead, the combat panel 0x554830 takes the image's place (OH7); else, when the PC detects alignment, the **alignment image** replaces the portrait and the type image: the stat's range (−1000..1000, tables 0x5B5194 and 0x5B5204) in six bands of a sixth (333), the band `(alignment + 1000) / 333` capped at 5, its art from table 0x5C6FCC (634, 390, 389, 388, 387, 386 from the evil end); otherwise the image of IW27. Then the name (title font, white, the title's line). **A dead critter** (0x45D8D0, hit points 0 or less) gets `intgame.mes` 16 on the second line (rectangle 0x5C70D8: 290, 528, 291 × 18, left-aligned, the red text font 0x64C49C) and the function ends: no level, no bars. **A living critter** gets, in the white text font (0x64C498), right-aligned on the second line, "label: level" (`%s: %d`, `intgame.mes` 0 and the level stat 17), and **an NPC only** (type 16) first a left-aligned part chosen by the PC: (1) PC concealed (0x45EDE0): "label: state" (`%s: %s`, `intgame.mes` 0x25 and a state line): the NPC's sight check toward the PC (0x4AF260, 0 = seen) and, when it fails, the smaller of its excess and the NPC's hearing check with loudness 0 (0x4AF470); a sleeping NPC (0x45D870) whose result is 0 counts as 1; the state line is `intgame.mes` 0x2A less the result for results 0..3 and 0x26 for any more; (2) else a PC that detects alignment: "label: N" (`%s: %d`, `intgame.mes` 0x24 and the alignment stat divided by 10, truncated toward zero); (3) else "label: R (category)" (`%s: %d (%s)`, `intgame.mes` 1, the standard effective reaction of the NPC toward the PC 0x4C0CC0, and the name of its category 0x4C0FC0, entry 0..6 of `mes\reaction.mes` through 0x4C1010). Then the bars (CH8). A PC's window has no left part. The window is drawn once when it opens. | Original; built by ARC-UI-109 (`AreaHoverWindow.CritterWindow`, `SneakStateMessage`, `AlignmentBand`, `AlignmentImage`; `AreaAiHost.StandardReaction`, `SneakExcess`; the draw `AreaMessageLayout.Draw`). The sight and hearing checks are the AI host's own, which does not feed the hero's prowling margin into them yet (so a concealed hero reads as the checks give without the margin); the PC's effective alignment and level are the stored stats (a follower's saved sheet level first), no effect changes |
| OH12 | item window 0x554F10 (called by 0x553BE0 for types 5–14 with the observer, the item and the name), item type icon 0x554C20, image 0x554560, name 0x441B60, identified test 0x464370, text draw 0x550A10 | Read on 2026-11-10 by ARC-UI-108 (the same executable, SHA-256 as in the [check](../original-executable-check.md#inputs); F1: one reader, who knew HK22's reading of the same function and found it as written; OH12–OH18 add the exact formats, rectangles, fonts, the owner rule and the helpers' reads). The second line (0x5C70D8: 290, 528, 291 × 18; font art 229, white, flag 1, left) is the effects text of the item (field 0x63 through 0x4D4080) when it is not empty and the item is not magickal (complexity 0 or less) or is identified; for a weapon or armour otherwise the line of OH14 or OH15. | Original |
| OH14 | weapon line 0x555910, segment builder 0x555780, formats 0x5C78A4, 0x5C78AC, 0x5C6218, 0x5C789C, 0x5C7898, 0x5B67C8 | Segments are appended in the order damage normal (`intgame.mes` 43), fatigue (44), to hit (45, from field 0x72, signed), range (46, field 0x79, nothing when it is 1), poison (47), fire (48), electrical (49), the damage ones from the arrays of fields 0x74 (lower) and 0x75 (upper) by the damage index (normal 0, poison 1, electrical 2, fire 3, fatigue 4). A segment is nothing when the value, the range and the bonus are all 0, else the label, a colon, then `%d-%d` when the upper value is not 0, else the single value (`%+d` for the signed ones: to hit, `%d` for the others; nothing when it is 0), then `(%+d)` when the bonus is not 0, then two spaces. The bonus is the magic adjustment (field 0x76 by index, 0x73, 0x7A) and is read only for an identified item with a positive complexity. | Original (built) |
| OH15 | armour line 0x555B50 | The same builder: armour class (50, field 0x98, bonus 0x99), the resistances from the arrays of fields 0x9A and 0x9B (signed) by the resistance index (normal 0 as 51, poison 3 as 52, fire 1 as 53, electrical 2 as 54, magic 4 as 55), the noise penalty (56, field 0x9C, bonus 0x9D, signed), and for armour that covers the hands (0x465C30: the inventory art's coverage 3) the unarmed damage bonus (field 0x9E) with label 43, signed. The bonuses are read under the same condition as OH14. Ammunition (6) and gold (8) draw `%s: %d` with `intgame.mes` 6 and the quantity field of 0x462410 (0x8E for ammunition, 0xA5 for gold) on the second line; a key (11) nothing; a key ring (12) `intgame.mes` 7 and the key count (0x463110: the count of the hovered ring's own key array, field 0xC2; Steamwoven counts the PC's key list, since the session keeps no ring items, so another ring lying about shows the PC's count); food, scroll, written and generic items the effects text under the condition of OH13, and a generic item (14) without a text, its usage bonus (field 0xD3 bit 0x2: 71 with field 0xD4; else bit 0x8: 72), `%s: %+d%%` (0x5C788C), also for an unidentified magickal one. | Original (built) |
| OH16 | third line 0x5C70E8 (290, 546), magick 0x4614A0, tech 0x461700, mana store field 0x69, ammunition 0x465CA0 and 0x4657D0 | For an identified weapon, armour or scroll (types 5, 7, 10) with a positive complexity the left part is `%s: %d%%` (0x5C7880, `intgame.mes` 2) with the power 0x4614A0 × 100 / complexity (white, flag 1); with a negative one `%s: %+d%%` (0x5C788C, 3) with the tech adjustment 0x461700: the owner's aptitude × complexity / 100 (0 without a positive aptitude), truncated toward zero. The right part (white, flag 2, right-aligned) is, for a positive complexity and charges (field 0x69) not 0, `intgame.mes` 19 with `%s: ??` while unidentified, else `%s: %d` or, when the value is 0 or less, `%s: +`; else for a generic item with flag 0x8 in field 0xD3 its uses (field 0xD5, label 19); else for a weapon with an ammunition type (0x465CA0, not 10000) and an ammunition per shot above 0 (field 0x7E) the ammunition's name (0x4657D0: `item.mes` 0–3) and the number as `%s: %d`. | Original (built; the charges follow the session's count) |
| OH17 | weight 0x461370, format 0x5C7864, hit points 0x43D600 and 0x43D5A0, 0x5045A0 | The fourth line (0x5C70F8) is drawn for every item: the weight (0x461370 with the owner, WE1–WE2) as `%s: %d %s` (`intgame.mes` 4 and 5) in red, left. Then, when the current art (field 1) is not a destroyed item art (0x5045A0: art type 6 with bit 0x400), a weapon's, an armour's or a generic item's (field 0xD3 bit 0x20) hit points `%d/%d` (0x5C7848, OH10's) in red, right-aligned. | Original (built) |
| OH18 | description loader 0x4D3F60 and 0x4D3FF0, lookup 0x4D4080, ammunition names 0x460EC0 | The effects text of a number 0..last is read from `mes\item_effect.mes` (the base file, read at game start) below 30000 and from the module's `gameitemeffect.mes` from 30000 up; a number past the last loaded, negative or without a message gives no text, as does an empty message (most items have none). The four ammunition names come from `mes\item.mes` 0–3 (read at game start). Installed texts read in the project's check: `intgame.mes` 2–8, 19, 43–56, 71, 72 (labels such as "D", "FT", "AC", "DR": the item window's lines are short forms), item_effect.mes 6054 "Damage:1-6 (Mana:10)". | Original (built; read through the content stack as `AreaItemIcons.EffectsText`, `AmmoName`) |
| OH19 | combat panel 0x554830 (called from 0x553F70, OH11), art draw 0x554B00, difficulty 0x4C8430, record setup 0x4C7090, cancel chance 0x461620, weapon skill 0x465D80, effectiveness 0x4C62E0 and 0x4C69F0, font build 0x549E49–0x549EBD, tables 0x5C732C (masks), 0x5C734C (arts), 0x5C736C and 0x5C7384 (places), format 0x5C7850 | Read on 2026-11-10 by ARC-UI-124 (F1: one reader, the retail 1.0.7.4 executable, SHA-256 `9bd80511…78d52` checked; the PR's independent review re-read it). **Draw.** The panel first draws art 0x246 (582 `Pen_BullsEye`, 80 × 89) at the window's (207, 57), which is (207, 498) on the screen (the window's origin is (0, 441)); with the interface hidden (0x5578C0) the draws go to another window shifted by (−210, −59) (not traced). 0x554B00 reads the art's frame data (0x5031C0); that the art's top left is the given place is inferred from the number's centre (the art is 80 wide, the number's rectangle 64 wide at 215: both centred at 247), not read. **Chance.** The weapon is the item 0x4649C0 gives for 0x3EC (1004, the weapon slot); its skill (0x465D80) decides: below 12 a basic skill (0x4C62E0 with the hero, the skill and the critter), else a tech skill (0x4C69F0 with the skill less 12). The invocation record (0x4C7090) is empty (flags 0, modifier 0, skill −1 then set; so no aim, no backstab, no bed, and the weapon's magic to-hit counts, SC5) and gets the hero, the critter, the weapon and the skill. The chance is the effectiveness less the total 0x4C8430 returns. With a weapon, and a skill other than throwing (3), the cancel chance (0x461620 with the weapon, the hero and the critter) above 0 takes its share: the chance less `chance × cancel / 100` toward zero (the compiler's multiplier 0xAE147AE1 is the division by −100); above 20 the panel sets its own flag 0x40000. A result below 0, or any of the flags 0x80 and 0x20000, gives 0; a chance above 100 is printed as it is. **Flags (0x4C8430, the record's word at +0x98).** Besides the invocation bits SC6–SC11 test, the function ORs one bit in for each term it adds: 0x40 the weapon's minimum strength above the source's strength (+5 a point, item type 5), 0x80 the target beyond the item's range (impossible, +1,000,000), 0x100 distance beyond half the perception below master (+5 a cell), 0x20000 a blocking object on the line of fire (impossible), else 0x200 a positive cover, 0x400 a darkness term, 0x800 blindness, crippled arms or crippled legs (30, 50 or 20, 30). **Layout.** With any of the mask 0x60FC0 set: the number `%d%%` (0x5C7850) in the font 0x64C4A0 (font art 0xE6 = 230, white, built at 0x549E49–0x549EBD like the others) centred across (0x550A10 flag 4) in the rectangle (215, 66, 64 × 64) of the window, (215, 507) on the screen, then each of the eight masks of 0x5C732C in order, when set, draws its art of 0x5C734C at the next of the six places (210, 84), (248, 84), (210, 104), (248, 104), (210, 124), (248, 124) (screen y + 441; the loop stops after six): 0x40 → 586 `Pen_MSR`, 0x80 → 588 `Pen_Range`, 0x100 → 587 `Pen_Perception`, 0x200 → 583 `Pen_Cover`, 0x400 → 585 `Pen_Light`, 0x800 → 584 `Pen_Injury`, 0x20000 → 845 `BlockedShot`, 0x40000 → 846 `Magic-Tech-Penalty` (names and sizes from the installed `interface.mes` and ART files: 38 × 20, 29 × 20, 34 × 20, 38 × 20, 27 × 20, 28 × 17, 37 × 20, 37 × 20). Without any of the mask: `intgame.mes` 20 ("To Hit:") in the text font 0x64C498 (229) centred in (215, 85, 64 × 64), and the number in the font 0x64C4A0 centred in the rectangle 0x12 lower, (215, 103). The panel runs for another critter when the hero is in combat mode and the critter is not dead (OH11); the window is drawn when the pointer enters the object, nothing redraws it while the pointer stays (inferred as OH10). | Original; built by ARC-UI-124 (`AttackChanceRules.Preview`, `SkillCheckRules.DifficultyReasons`, `AreaCombatPanelLayout`; the art placement, the hidden-interface offsets, the hot-spot handling and the undefined-distance exit are not read or not modelled)

## Menu window art (ARC-UI-058)

Source: [FR-UI-001](../../requirements/ui-media-lifecycle.md#fr-ui-001), the owner's bug report of 2026-10-29 (the main menu and
its windows drew the project's panel, not the game's art); plan [original windows](../../ui/original-windows.md#main-menu-done-arc-ui-058).
Read on 2026-10-29 by this task in the window table 0x5C3A40 (descriptors as in [MM1](#main-menu-arc-ui-011)), the window build
0x546340, the label draw 0x546B40, the menu's font build (0x540A22..0x540B90), the text size 0x535390 with the glyph metrics
0x535D10, the frame info 0x5031C0 and the menu event handler 0x546EE0. Art names are `art\interface\interface.mes`'s, sizes the
installed arts'. Coordinates are the 800 × 600 screen's. Every direct branch stays inside `.text`. The rows are F1 (one reader).

For each glyph 0x535570 hands the art blit a descriptor with the font's colour (font +8) and flags 0x2000, plus 0x10 when the font has flag 0x100 and 0x200 when it lacks flag 0x80 (a shadow pass, font flag 0x8, draws first at (+1, +1) in the shadow colour 0x630D50). Flags beyond the mirror bits go to the hardware blit only on a 3D surface (0x520FB0); otherwise the software renderer draws the glyph, skipping palette index 0, by the first test that holds: flag 0x10 adds glyph × colour to the screen channel by channel, held at the channel's top; flag 0x200 takes the glyph pixel's red field as the cover a = red × 255 / red's top (rounded down; table 0x739EA0, entry 0 of 0x52C070 is the red mask, its caller packing 0xFF, 0xFF00, 0xFF0000) and makes each channel screen + (colour − screen) × a, shifted right by 8; with neither it writes glyph × colour (opaque). A product is i × j / the channel's top, rounded down (table 0x739EA4). The 16-, 24- and 32-bit screens do the same on their own fields (the 24/32-bit red field is 8 bits, so a is red). The font build 0x5352E0 copies the descriptor (flags +0, art +4, colour +8) and bakes nothing; the menu's font build 0x540A22..0x540FB0 gives the second font of each Morph30 set (the brown) flag 0x100 and every other font it makes (the Morph30 sets' black and face, MW3; the art 27 and 171 fonts, MW14) flags 0. | Original; built by ARC-UI-070: the shared art-text draw (`AreaArtTextRules`, `AreaArtTextDraw`) used by the menus, options, save and load, pre-made and new-character windows and the dialog box (PROJECT: the canvas mixes by w / 255 with w = a × 255 / 256 rounded, within one level of the original's; the screen keeps 8-bit channels) |

## World map window (ARC-UI-073)

Source: a static reading of the world-map window of the retail 1.0.7.4 `Arcanum.exe` (interface mode 5) with Xcode `llvm-objdump`, read on
2026-11-07, one reader (method step 6, the independent second reading, is still owed); the art's geometry was read in the installed `mapmain`
(art 138). Message numbers are of `mes\WorldMap.mes` (arcanum3.dat), art numbers of `art\interface\interface.mes`. Names: the **view kind**
(0x66D868) indexes three 704-byte entries at 0x5C9228: kind 0 "Map View" (the 2000 x 2000 tiled picture), kind 1 "Continent View"
(`Map_Zoomed`), kind 2 the town map; the **tool state** (0x66D8AC) is 0 waypoint, 1 dragging a waypoint, 2 travelling, 3 note quill,
4 compass scroll. The window is 800 x 400 at the screen's (0, 41) (table 0x5C9B00). **Coordinates.** The module init 0x55F8D0 subtracts the window's 41 rows from the draw rectangles once (WM40): the view's rectangle (150, 52, 501, 365)
of the screen is (150, 11) in the window, exactly the transparent hole of art 138 (x 150..650, y 11..375, read in the art), and the hit rectangles stay
in screen coordinates while the converters add the 41 back, so the clickable area is the drawn view (WM41). Rows WM1–WM39 were written before this was
known: where one says "window (150, 52)", "window (218, 52)" or "window (707, 200)" it means the screen's, and WM25, WM26 and WM35 are corrected by WM41
and WM51. The buttons' positions are given as screen coordinates and are window coordinates after subtracting 41. Rows WM1–WM39 are the first reading,
WM40–WM64 the second (the area labels, the readouts, the continent view, the hover texts and the buttons). Built by ARC-UI-073 (the window, the picture, the
scroll, the markers) and ARC-UI-083 (names, readouts, the continent view, the hover texts); ARC-UI-084 and ARC-UI-085 follow.

| id | address | behaviour | verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| WM1 | 0x560760 | The `W` open (also the bar's Map button and four other callers) clears the scripted-open state (spell value 0x5C9220 = -1, caster handle 0x66D888/0x66D88C = 0, scripted flag 0x66D9C8 = 0) and calls the common open 0x5607E0. | Not a bug |
| WM2 | 0x560790 | The scripted open (the only other caller of 0x5607E0; its address is stored by 0x57C8A1) takes a caster handle and a value kept in 0x5C9220, clears the waypoint count 0x64E408 and 0x65E968, sets the scripted flag and clears the walkable flag 0x66D880, then calls 0x5607E0. | Not a bug |
| WM3 | 0x5607E0 | A second open while the window is open (0x66D864) closes it (0x560F40), so `W` toggles. Without a PC nothing happens; two PC checks (0x45D8D0, 0x45D800, F0) silently refuse. Refusals with a line in the message window (type 6): the encounter flag 0x66D9C4 gives 602; a PC sector in the scripts' blocked-sector list (0x4D0EE0) gives 605; a PC in combat (0x4B3D50) that cannot leave it (0x4B3D90) gives 600, one that can leaves combat first (0x4B3F20). | Not a bug |

| WM106 | 0x44EB40, 0x426560, 0x426320, 0x5E33F8 | Town path test: 0x44EB40 fails for a null PC, a null target or a target whose two halves are both −1; it writes flag word 1 into the static request 0x5E33F8 and 200 into its +0xCC, and calls 0x426560(PC, start, target, request, 0). +0xCC is a straight-distance threshold: when the distance (0x4B96F0) exceeds 200 only a log line "Estimated Distance is too large" follows; the search budget +0xE0 becomes 4 × distance + 5 when it is 0, else grows by 5 up to +0xE4. | WM108 | 0x433A00, 0x44DBE0 | 0x433A00(PC, location, flag): network client → packet 0x28 subtype 2. Single player: with no running goal 3 and flag 0 → a fresh goal 3 started exactly as 0x433640; with a running goal 3 (or flag ≠ 0) whose target differs → a goal 3 for the location is appended to that run (0x44DBE0); same target → nothing.
