| WM110 | 0x4BE780, 0x58AF74 | Town pixel → full-picture offset. Descriptor fields: +0x0C picture width W, +0x10 height H, +0x20/+0x24 origin location (x, y), +0x28 scale (float). The pixel (px, py) is converted to floating point, divided by the scale and converted back with truncation toward zero (0x58AF74 forces the chop rounding mode, 64-bit result); W/2 and H/2 are signed halvings truncating toward zero. u = trunc(px/scale) − W/2, v = trunc(py/scale) − H/2. With scale 0.25 the division is exact, so u = 4·px − W/2. The output location is pre-zeroed, then overwritten unconditionally from a local after the conversion. | Not a bug |
| WM111 | 0x4B8680, 0x4B8730 | The origin's screen point is taken from the location → screen function (iso mode, 0x5FC2D0 = 0): sx = (oy − ox − 1)·40 + view x (0x5FC2E0), sy = (ox + oy)·20 + view y (0x5FC2E8); the clicked screen point is (u + sx, v + sy) and goes to screen → location, where the view origin cancels. There: a = floor((X − view x)/2) (64-bit arithmetic shift), b = Y − view y; tile x from b − a, tile y from b + a, each divided by 40 as "truncate toward zero, then −1 when negative" (floor except an exact negative multiple of 40, one lower; only reachable for negative results). It fails (returns 0, writes nothing) when b − a < −2³¹, when x < 0 or x ≥ the current map's width (0x5FC2F0/F4), or y < 0 or y ≥ its height (0x5FC288/8C). No clamp to the town picture: any pixel inside the current map's bounds yields a tile, even outside the drawn town. Final formula in item 1 of the report. | Not a bug |
| WM112 | 0x4BE780, 0x4B8730 | On a failed conversion 0x4BE780 still copies its output local: low half = an uninitialised stack word, high half = the high dword of trunc(py/scale) (0 for py ≥ 0). Reachable only for a town at the edge of the current map (F1). | Technical bug candidate (read of an uninitialised value) |
| WM113 | 0x4BE780, 0x4B8680 | The forward conversion (known: full = (W/2 + dx, H/2 + dy)) gives a tile's left diamond corner; the inverse takes the diamond whose centre is that corner + (40, 20) full pixels (+(10, 5) picture pixels at scale 0.25). Converting a forward pixel back gives (lx, ly − 1); a click at corner + (10, 5) picture pixels gives (lx, ly). The stored pixel is the click pixel, so the original never round-trips. | Not a bug (port note) |
| WM114 | 0x44EB40 | Corrects WM106: refuses a null PC, a target whose two halves are both 0 (tile (0, 0), "null location") or both −1. | WM115 | 0x426320 | Budget (max path length in steps) from the request: +0xCC = T (200 here). +0xE0 = 0: d = straight distance (0x4B96F0); d > T only logs ("Estimated Distance is too large" and two more lines), never refuses; +0xE0 = 4d + 5; +0xE4 lowered to T if above. +0xE0 ≠ 0: +0xE0 += 5; above +0xE4 → +0xE0 = +0xE4 when +0xE4 > 0, else +0xE4 = min(4d + 5, T) is computed once and +0xE0 lowered to it. Always +0xE0 ≤ T; the result is +0xE0. For the static request (never reset, bss 0): call 1 budget = min(4d₁ + 5, 200); call 2 sets +0xE4 = min(4d₂ + 5, 200) and budget = min(budget₁ + 5, +0xE4); from call 3 on budget = min(previous + 5, the +0xE4 of call 2) for the rest of the process, whatever the new distance. A far waypoint after near ones can be refused for budget (610) although a path exists. | Technical bug candidate (stale search budget; refines WM106) |
| WM116 | 0x426560 | Builds the search record {PC, start, target, budget, path buffer = request + 4 (200 bytes), flags}. Flag 0x1000 in the caller's flags forces budget 200 (not here). Bits from the PC: field 0x14 bit 0x20000 → 0x6; 0x45F550 = 0 → 0x2; 0x45F570 = 0 → 0x4 (art class test, F1); 0x45EDE0 (critter field 0xDA bit 0) and 0x4C60C0(PC, 6) ≤ 0 → 0x200. When the PC's fields 3/4 (sub-tile offsets, F1) are not both 0, the start becomes the neighbour tile in the PC's facing direction (field 1's rotation), the budget drops by 1 and the first path byte is that direction; a blocked neighbour refuses (this applies to the start of every waypoint test, also when the start is a previous waypoint: minor, F1). Then 0x425BF0(record, 1) and the search 0x41F3C0. Success: request +0xD8 = path length, +0xD4 = 0, word 0 &= ~3, +0xE8 = start, +0xF0 = target; returns 1. Failure: +0xD8 = 0; for a non-PC object 0x4B7C90 (F0); returns 0. No PC field, goal or run is written; only the static request. | Not a bug |
| WM117 | 0x425BF0 | Not the search: only for a PC or NPC (field 0x14F = 15/16). | WM118 | 0x41F3C0 | Search dispatcher: flag 0x800/0x80/0x100 → other searchers (not set here); start = target → 0 (fail): a town waypoint on the PC's tile or on the previous waypoint's tile gets 610. | WM119 | 0x41F9F0, 0x4200C0 | Grid best-first (A*-like) search over the current map: a 64 × 64 tile window placed around start and target; it fails at once when start and target differ by 32 or more tiles on either axis (so a town waypoint ≥ 32 tiles from the previous one on x or y always gets 610, whatever the budget). 8 neighbours; the open node with the lowest g + h is picked by a linear scan; h = Chebyshev distance in tiles (unscaled against step costs in tenths, so nearly Dijkstra). Step cost 10; 90 when the tile holds a portal/door the walker cannot pass freely (0x4BCAB0 → 0x4BBFE0 ≠ 0, F1); +50 when the object test 0x43FDC0 reports a passable obstacle (F1); +80 with flag 0x400 when 0x444500 finds something on the tile (F1); +40 uphill by more than 20 height units, −6 downhill by more than 20 (0x4D9240); +1 for a change of direction. Blocked: 0x4D7110 tiles (unless 0x8), tiles 0x43FDC0 blocks (for diagonal directions only when its reason code is not 0/1, F1). A node with (g + h)/10 above the budget is closed; a path longer than the budget fails. NPCs (type 16) are throttled per frame (0x5DE5FC/0x5DE600 vs 0x5A15C0/C4), the PC never. | Not a bug |

Leaving: `Q`, the right release and Delete (WM98), the Travel button (WM89), a kind switch (WM103), a closed window. Entering clears the waypoint selection (WM103) and the marker hover is off (WM59). A note is drawn with art 143 centred on its point, after the area markers and labels and under the route (map view) or after the red dots (continent view). Hit boxes: ±9 picture pixels in the map view (the markers' box, WM58); in the continent view, where the picture is shrunk to 365 px and art 143 is drawn at full size, at least the markers' ±20 and at least the drawn art's half size in picture pixels (so a press on the visible edge of a note edits it); the newest note wins. With the quill off a note shows its text in the message window where no area marker lies under the pointer (a marker's description wins); with the quill on both hovers are off (WM59; the area markers' descriptions too, a project choice: the town map has no area markers). Leaving the quill is always allowed; entering is not while a journey runs, and Enter or the confirm pulse that starts a journey leaves it as the Travel button does. Entering clears the waypoint selection and keeps the waypoints (WM103); a press in the quill state starts no waypoint and no drag, and a release in the continent view does not zoom in (WM34); the right release, Delete, the Travel button, a kind switch and a closed window leave it (WM98, WM89, WM103). | WM133 | 0x44DD80, 0x424D90, 0x426040, 0x425740, 0x430F20, 0x423D10–0x423D79, 0x423560 | Read in ARC-DEV-311 (one Opus reader, llvm-objdump only, F1): **a town route is one run whose goal-3 frames each walk to their own tile.** The parameter set-up always reads the run's current (top) frame, so the exact-arrival test (goal 3 node 1) and the planner use the top frame's destination; only the starters read the bottom frame (+0x68/+0x6C). Goal 5 has no destination of its own: it walks the run's path planned by the goal 3 below it. When waypoint k arrives, node 1 pops goal 3 (its end node 0x42B090 resets the art to animation 0 frame 0), the pop zeroes the run's budget and clears run flags 0x4, 0x8, 0x10, 0x20 and 0x800 but **keeps 0x40 (running) and 0x400 (straight line)**, and leaves the path flags alone. Every segment ended with goal 5's end node 0x430F20 setting "needs plan" (+0xC00 bit 0x1), so goal 3 for waypoint k+1 restarts at node 1, refuses the old path (node 4, 0x425740) and plans afresh from the hero's tile with a zero budget: no stale path leaks into the next leg. Popping the bottom goal 3 (the last waypoint) marks the run done. | Not a bug |
| WM134 | 0x433640 (0x433800–0x433876), 0x425430, 0x4254C0, 0x42CA90, 0x433C80 (0x433DEA–0x433E3C), 0x44E830 | A ground click, a held-stick repeat or a second Travel's first waypoint while a route runs finds the run (the goal-3 finder, aliases 7 and 4; the run starter's finder uses 7, 3 and 55) and **overwrites only the bottom frame's destination**, the last waypoint, and ORs 0x4 into +0xC00; the top waypoint's target is unchanged (clicking a tile equal to an intermediate waypoint replaces the last one just the same). The bit is consumed only by goal 5's node 5 (0x425430): it sets "needs plan" and pops goal 5. With goal 3 on top, node 4 pushes goal 5 and it pops at once; with goal 5 mid-segment the step under way completes (node 1 tests run flag 0x10), then goal 5 pops at the next step boundary instead of finishing its segment, and goal 3 replans to its **own** tile: the click costs one replan of the current leg and the final tile becomes the click. Neither a walk click on a running route nor a run click on a walking one interrupts or starts a fresh run while a match exists; a run click ORs 0x40 onto the found run, so the whole rest of the route runs. Confirms WM124. | Gameplay flaw candidate (the route keeps going and ends on the clicked tile, F1) |
| WM135 | 0x4246D0, 0x426040 (0x42614A), 0x4270B0, 0x433270, 0x42E9B0, 0x425495–0x4254A7, 0x4305D0, 0x423560 | **Every failure of the PC's walk ends the whole run**, queued waypoints included (the end step marks the run done, runs the end nodes on frames 1..top and then frame 0, and the free path runs them again). Goal 3/4: node 5 (plan) false goes to node 7: outside a round it pushes goal 6 for 1000 ms and goal 6 ends the run, in a round it ends the run at once. Goal 5: the block test (node 2) true goes to node 10 after a random 0–300 ms and node 10 (0x433270) ends the run; the step start 0x42E9B0 false (node 9; run flag 0x100, stoned, 0xDA & 0x60) goes to node 10, **not to a restart** (corrects GT148); node 4 with the path bit 0x1 set false goes to node 12, the no-path end; node 11 false ends the run (true pushes 14, the window jump); node 5 ends the run in single player when +0xC00 bit 0x8 is set (its setter not read). A failed action-point payment self-interrupts at level 6 (GS20), which also ends the whole run. Node 3's move step false is a pop at the segment's end, not a failure. With flag 0x400 set a failed plan returns at once (0x42614A), so a leg that needs a detour **ends the route**. The existing statement that a refused or blocked step ends the run is confirmed for a multi-frame run. | Not a bug |
| WM136 | 0x44E2C0 (GT8), 0x424070, 0x4246E0, 0x44E4D0 | The route's goals (3, 4, 5, 6, 53) all have priority 2, so any level-3 interrupt ends the whole run: an object click (GS27), the right release (GT101), and the movement, attack, throw and follow starters (GS20). Two exceptions come from the **current** goal (GS16): while goal 3's node-3 push 54 (`ag_unconceal`, priority 4) is on top, level 3 is refused (0x424070 answers 0; the right release then skips its tail); while the door goal 12 (no-interrupt flag, reached through goal 5 node 8 → 11 → 12) is on top, the interrupt reports success and leaves the route running (a new goal then starts as a second run of the hero, inferred from GS16, not traced). The bottom of a town route is goal 3 even when waypoint 0 runs (goal 4 on top), so the interrupt-by-goal 0x44E4D0(goal 3) of an entangle or don't-draw ends a running town route though it does not end a single run click (inferred). The cast and skill starters' interrupt levels were not read (F0). | Not a bug |
| WM137 | 0x44D9B0, 0x44DBE0, 0x44EEE0, 0x423893–0x42398B | A second Travel: 0x433640(waypoint 0) retargets the bottom to waypoint 0, then each further waypoint goes through 0x433A00 (0x433BBC–0x433C4E), which compares with the bottom (the newest insert), skips an equal tile, and otherwise builds a goal-3 frame (0x44D500) and inserts it at the bottom: the route doubles. **Stack overflow:** with the top index already 7 the single-player insert only logs the assert "current_goal < (ANIM_MAX_GOAL_STACK_SIZE - 1)" (0x44EEE0 dumps the table, no abort), increments the index, shifts every frame up and writes the new frame 0 anyway: the ninth frame (+0xBF8..+0xD6F) overwrites the run's own current-goal pointer, path flags and path (+0xC00..), budget (+0xCE0), pace (+0xCFC) and work area (+0xD00..), plus the first 0x38 bytes of the **next slot's** record (id, flags, node index, owner, top index); frames 10 and up lie wholly in the next slot. A push from top index 7 or more (goal 5 on top of eight goals) logs "Goal Stack too LARGE… Killing the Animation" and marks the run done, which is the engine's own guard: a route of eight goals with goal 3 on top cannot push goal 5 and dies at once. Extends WM123 and GS14. | Technical bug (memory corruption; never reproduced); the guard is Original behaviour |
| WM138 | 0x42614A, 0x433C80, 0x434030, 0x433DEA–0x433E3C | Gait and the straight-line retry belong to the **run**, not to a leg: once one leg needed the PC's straight-line retry (0x400), or a held walk marked it, every later leg plans only the straight line, and a later leg that needs a detour ends the route; run flag 0x40 likewise lasts for the whole route. A run click on a walking route sets 0x40 even for an overloaded hero (the dead encumbrance test sits in the found-run branch of 0x433C80 too, beside WM122). | Gameplay flaw candidate (F1) |
| WM139 | not read | Not read in ARC-DEV-311 (F0): 0x430FC0 inside goal 5 node 5, the setter of +0xC00 bit 0x8, 0x4254C0 (it sets 0x4 when +0xD08 differs from the top frame's +0x30; which goal runs it is unknown), the return cases of 0x4305D0, whether the stand-frame reset of goal 3's end node at every waypoint is visible, the interrupt levels of the cast and skill starters, and the right button *press* in the game view on a running route (the game view's input; the right *release* is read: WM136). | not read |

Not resolved here (F0): the button frames, hit rectangles and flag meanings (0x537B50); the sound named by 0x41B780(0, 1) and sound ids 3000/3001; the
control that enters the note quill (WM30); the meaning of font flag 0x80, the font's
line height and the use of arts 196 and 197 (WM46, WM64); the view's behaviour while travelling (WM18 and WM20 stop the scroll then, nothing else is read).
WM110–WM125 (ARC-UI-092) read the town pixel → location conversion, the town path test and the PC's walk along the waypoints; they correct WM106 (WM114) and WM89 and WM107 (WM121: the first walk is a ground click's, with its gait).
WM95–WM109 (ARC-UI-085) read the waypoint lists, the hit box, the drag and release, the deletion and the town add and walk; they correct WM29 (the drag does not move a
waypoint), WM33, WM87 and WM89 (above).
WM42–WM44 closed the art at 0x5C9B48 (`Nav_Cvr`) and the fill colour (black), WM41 the mouse frame. WM65–WM94 (ARC-UI-084) read the town map (kind 2)
and the route drawing; they correct WM38: the town map is a tiled picture chosen by the sector's town-map number, not an interface art of `WorldMap.mes`
501–503, and WM83: the extra art of the kind switch is the kind-art slot, which is −1. Left unresolved there: WM68 (the user of the seven 668 × 400 map
arts), WM79 (the intent of the one note art), WM89 (0x436D20 and single-player running), WM90 (0x4607B0), WM93 (the caller), WM80 (the line state's
meaning) and WM78 (the PC checks of the reveal caller).

**Decisions of ARC-UI-084** (AGENTS.md original bugs): WM73 (a failed tile 000000 load is not fatal and leaves the tile size 0) is a technical flaw
whose case cannot occur here, the viewer refusing a town whose tile 000000 does not read and falling back to the map view, as WM72 does; WM75 (a switch
to the town map leaks the earlier tile array and its buffers) is technical and not reproduced, the viewer frees its textures; WM81 (a member outside the
picture is drawn at its corner) and WM79 (every note drawn with one art) are presentation flaws left to the slices that draw members and notes. WM86
(the first press of the session in the town map is dead) and WM91 (an unchecked text length in a note file) are for the slices of the town waypoints and
notes (ARC-UI-092 and ARC-UI-093).

**Decisions of ARC-UI-085** (AGENTS.md original bugs): WM97 is the one gameplay flaw: deleting a waypoint that is not the last only shifts the list, so the
journey still walks the deleted waypoint's steps and only the stop is dropped, while the drawn line goes straight to the next one. It is the travel switch
`DeletedWaypointKeepsSteps` (rule modes A5): Original keeps it, Steamwoven plans the following segments again from the waypoint before (or the map position) and,
when one of them has no route, keeps the waypoint and says so (PROJECT). WM99 (dragging never moves a waypoint, the original's move is an empty function) is kept in
both modes: the drag only scrolls the view near its edges, and the release snaps the waypoint to a known area (WM100). WM98 (a right release deletes wherever it is,
even during a drag) and WM101 (the armed flag) are kept. The route is drawn as the original does, straight lines through the waypoints' icon pixels (WM104), not the
sector steps. Not read (F0): the sizes of arts 205 and 206 (the hit box uses 205's, as the original does), the line style states of WM104, the right release in the
continent view (the viewer acts in the map view only), and the town waypoint list (ARC-UI-092). WM86 (the first press of the session in the town map is dead) stays for ARC-UI-092.

**Decisions of ARC-UI-092** (AGENTS.md original bugs; owner rule 2026-10-28): WM86 (the town armed flag starts clear, so the first press of a session is dead) is a technical
flaw fixed in both rule modes: the window opens armed. WM112 (a click whose conversion fails keeps an uninitialised location) and WM115 (the stale search budget, a far
waypoint after near ones can be refused although a path exists) are technical flaws not reproduced: a failed conversion is a blocked path (message 610), and each path test has its
own budget. The path test itself is the original's in both modes: start and target differ, both axes less than 32 tiles apart, the PC's own walk plan (LR2 budget, no
corridor, no straight-line retry) finds a path (WM118, WM119, WM125); whether the original also refuses a target holding a creature, container or scenery is F1 (the
planner's own blockers apply). Travel walks the PC to the first waypoint as a ground click does and each further waypoint is stacked as a goal-3 walk in the same run of the hero's table since ARC-DEV-311 and ARC-DEV-322 (WM107, WM108, WM123); WM122 (waypoints after the first always run, an overloaded PC included) is a gameplay flaw candidate at F1 and is not reproduced: every walk takes the ground
click's gait. What a failed or replaced walk does to the goals beneath it is read in ARC-DEV-311 (WM133–WM139): every failure and every other action that interrupts ends the whole route run, in both rule modes, and a ground click or the stick overwrites only the route's last waypoint; the route is that one run in both rule modes, with no switch (ARC-DEV-322, the paragraph "Decisions of ARC-DEV-311"). WM123's frame stack overflow is never reproduced: a ninth frame is dropped. WM108's skipped first waypoint (a refused start for waypoint 0 lets waypoint 1 start a fresh walk, a gameplay flaw candidate at F1) is reproduced in both modes until the owner decides otherwise; a walk already heading for the first waypoint counts as started. The list is kept by the host until another town, map or session replaces it (WM89: the original clears it at the next load).

**Decisions of ARC-UI-093** (AGENTS.md original bugs): WM81 (a follower outside the picture is drawn at its top-left corner) is a presentation glitch not reproduced in
either rule mode: such a follower is left out of the town map; the others are drawn at their current pixels with the party art, after the party marker. WM126 (the
note quill is unreachable) is a technical bug of the original, a dead feature; whether the project adds a quill of its own is the owner's call (not built by this task).
WM79 (every note drawn with the question art 143, whatever its type) is kept: only type 3 notes exist (WM127), so no other type is drawn in practice. ARC-UI-103 builds the notes: the hover mark (WM127, a scenery outside the town's picture adds no note instead of the corner note of the converter's (0, 0), a presentation glitch not reproduced), the cap of 199 and the ids from 200 (WM128), the drawing (WM79, WM82) and the hover text (WM59); an edited file's overlong text and a missing text buffer are for ARC-UI-104. WM91's unchecked
text length of a `.tmn` file is a robustness flaw not reproduced (ARC-UI-104).

**Decisions of ARC-UI-106** (owner decision 2026-11-08, variant B1): the original's quill is dead code (WM126), so the Steamwoven-only quill (WM131) is a project addition and exists in both rule modes as an input convenience (it changes no rule). It works in the town map only: the map view and the continent view keep no notes in the session, so notes there would need a second list and a schema step, left to a task of their own if wanted.

**Decisions of ARC-UI-110** (owner decision 2026-11-08, variant B1): the Steamwoven-only quill (WM131) also works in the map view and the continent view (WM132). Each of the two views keeps its own list in the session (`story.mapNotes`, schema 82), so a note made in the continent view does not show in the map view and the other way round: the continent picture is a shrunk copy of the map one (365 px for the whole width), and one shared list would put a note made there at a coarse pixel into the map view. The input convenience changes no rule and exists in both rule modes.

**Decisions of ARC-UI-105** (AGENTS.md original bugs; owner rule 2026-10-28): the right release in the continent view is built (WM130). The sizes of arts 205 and 206 are measured (WM129) and the hit-box question of WM102 is closed. The goal stack under a failed or replaced town walk (WM123, WM124) stays the project's choice from ARC-UI-092 (a walk replaced by a click or the stick, or ended short of its tile, drops the rest of the route) and stays F0: it needs a read of the goal engine's frame-failure rule for the PC's queued walks and the PC's run record (8 frames), and it is a gameplay flaw candidate (the original keeps the route and ends at the clicked tile, WM124), so it would bring an Original switch; it is left to a task of its own, not folded into this one. (Superseded: ARC-DEV-311 read it and built the original's route as an Original switch, and ARC-DEV-322 made it the one route of both settings, no switch.) The right button on a running route belongs to the game view's input (WM130).

**Decisions of ARC-DEV-311** (AGENTS.md original bugs; owner rule 2026-10-28; the read is WM133–WM139): the executable settles what ARC-UI-105 left at F0. (1) A failed leg, an interrupt by any other action, a refused step or a path that cannot be found end the **whole** route run in both rule modes (no switch: this is the original and the project's earlier choice already). (2) The route is built as the original does, one run of the hero's table with a goal-3 walk per waypoint (`AreaHeroRunsHost.AppendWalk`, `GameSessionOwner.InsertGoalAtBottom`), so a ground click or a held stick overwrites only the last waypoint (the hero finishes the walks above it and ends on the clicked tile) and a second Travel overwrites the last waypoint with its first and stacks the rest again (the route doubles). ARC-DEV-311 first made this the Original setting beside a Steamwoven chain of single walks (switch `InterfaceRuleProfile.TownRouteIsOneRun`); **ARC-DEV-322** (owner decision A, 2026-11-10, D9: no switch) removed the switch and the chain (`AreaTownWalkChain`), so the one-run route applies in both settings. (3) WM137's stack overflow is a memory corruption and is never reproduced: a ninth waypoint is dropped (`HeroWalkAppendResult.StackFull`), and a route of eight goals with goal 3 on top ends at its first push, as the original's own guard does. (4) The gait and the straight-line mark are the run's (WM138) in the Original route; a run click on a walking route does not set the run flag (the project gives every walk the ground click's gait, WM122), an overloaded hero included, in both modes. (5) GT148's "a paralysed or stoned runner freezes and keeps its run" is corrected by WM135 (the run ends); the entangled-hero decision of ARC-DEV-188 is untouched, a second reader is owed.

**Decisions of ARC-WORLD-122** (AGENTS.md original bugs): the walked cell is the one tile under the PC (WM78) and the session keeps its bit per town map (schema 75,
ledger UF4); a PC whose location lies outside the town's picture reveals nothing, where the original's converter leaves (0, 0) and sets the corner tile (a technical
fog glitch, not reproduced in either rule mode). The viewer watches the hero's committed cell once per frame instead of the original's call on every location change
(the same result for a walk, a teleport or a load); the PC checks of the original's caller (WM78) are not read, so any PC the session holds is treated as the local one.

**Decisions of ARC-UI-083** (owner rule 2026-10-28): WM17 and WM57 (only the first eight markers show as dots in the continent view) are technical
presentation flaws and are fixed in both rule modes: every marker shows; WM43 and WM48 are latent or dead work and are not reproduced; WM50 (an id equal to
the area count reads one past the offset arrays) cannot occur here, the markers being areas below the count. WM63 (the zoom and whole buttons work during a
journey and end it, the effect taken from WM23, F1) is not reproduced: the viewer guards the buttons as it guards Z; whether the original's behaviour is a
gameplay switch is left open until 0x5615D0(0) is read. The 1 px off-centre of the last discovered area's and the party's squares (WM55, WM56) is kept.

**Decisions of ARC-UI-073** (AGENTS.md original bugs; owner rule 2026-10-28): WM13 (centring near the east or south edge shows blank fill),
WM17 (only the first eight markers show in the continent view) and WM21 (an arrow scrolls through three paths at different rates) are technical
flaws and are fixed in both rule modes without a switch; the viewer keeps one scroll path, the window's own poll of WM18 (20 px, at most every
10 ms). WM8 and WM24 are dead paths. WM26 and WM27 stay unresolved; the viewer picks the pixel under the pointer.

## Screen size and layout (ARC-RES-093)

Source: the [screen size and UI layout](../screen-size-and-ui-layout.md) research for the scalable HD interface (FR-UI-007):
where the windows sit, and which game rules read the screen or play-view size. Read on 2026-11-07 in the GOG 1.0.7.4
`Arcanum.exe` (SHA-256 prefix 9bd80511f8663a41) with Capstone in the Linux VM on the owner's Mac; data words from `.data`.
Coordinates are the 800 × 600 screen's. A window rectangle from 0x51CEB0 is its flags, x, y, width and height. The rows are
F1 (one reader); rows that only cite earlier rows keep those rows' levels.

| ID | Function (address) | Original 1.0.7.4 | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SZ1 | game window 0x557810 (from 0x4012C2), width 0x5C739C, height 0x5C73A0, hide state 0x5578C0; switch scan 0x4011F5 → 0x5578D0, 0x401206 → 0x5578E0; window create 0x51CB90, rectangle getter 0x51CEB0 | The game window is made once at start: x 0, width 800; y 41 and height 400 while the interface is shown, y 0 and height 600 (0x5C73A0) while it is hidden. It starts hidden only when the command line has `-fullscreen`: the scan allows the toggle (0x5578D0) and toggles once before any window exists (0x5578E0 then finds no window and returns). A window's rectangle is written only by the create (it copies the descriptor; no other code writes the window record's rectangle), so the game window never moves or resizes in a run: 800 × 400 at (0, 41) in the default launch, 800 × 600 at (0, 0) under `-fullscreen`. The GOG launcher entries pass no argument (normal) and `-no3d -doublebuffer` (Safe Mode). | Original |
| SZ2 | start 0x401229..0x401266 (library init 0x51F130), game library init 0x4020F0 (0x4021C7..0x4022C6), buffer 0x5200F0 | The window library is started with an 800 × 600 screen at 16 bits; no resolution or window setting is read. The game library takes the game window's rectangle (init record +4 is its handle) and stores the view rectangle (0, 0, W, H) at 0x5D0018–0x5D0024, a margin rectangle (−256, −256, W + 512, H + 512) at 0x5D0D60–0x5D0D6C, W / 4 and H / 4 at 0x5D10C0 and 0x5D0D80 (the save preview's size, SL8: 200 × 100 by default), and makes a W × H buffer. | Original |
| SZ3 | hide toggle 0x5578E0 (both calls of 0x4025C0 at 0x5579E9, 0x557A52), resize 0x4025C0, UI fan-out 0x53E700; tables: game library 0x59A330 (0x2C bytes an entry, resize at +0x28), UI 0x5C2AE0 (0x24 bytes, resize at +0x20), map 0x59F064 (0x34 bytes, resize at +0x34, from Map's resize 0x40ECE0); round view squares 0x5C63B0, 0x5C63C0 | The toggle (allowed only under `-fullscreen`, PA4) is the only caller of the resize. It passes the game window handle, a rectangle and the new view rectangle: hiding passes the window's own rectangle for both (800 × 600 at (0, 0)); showing passes height 400 and the view (0, 41, W, 400). It also moves the round view's square from (311, 96, 178 × 178) to (311, 196) while hidden. The resize stores the view (0x5D0018–0x5D0024, the margin, 0x5D0D78–0x5D0D7C), rebuilds the buffer, calls the resize of each game-library module (Map, Facade, the dialog options module `TC`, Item, Tile-Script, `WP`, GFade, `LI`, TileBlock, MonsterGen) and then each UI module's (Intgame, Hotkey-UI, Target, Iso, Combat-UI, Follower-UI). Map's resize calls its sub-modules' (Scroll, Location, Light, Tile, Roof, Object, Sector, text bubbles, floaters, Wall, JumpPoint). | Original |
| SZ4 | inits that read the game window: Scroll 0x40DF50, Location 0x4B8440, Object 0x43A330, Item 0x460E70, MonsterGen 0x4BA380, indicators 0x4BB720 / 0x4BB9A0 / 0x4BBB20, dialog options 0x4C9280, floaters 0x4D4E20, bubbles 0x4D5B80, Light 0x4D7F30, Wall 0x4DF390, Target 0x4F24F0, Iso 0x557DF0, Intgame 0x54A330, Combat-UI 0x56EA90; their resizes (SZ3) | Each stores the window's size at init and the view rectangle at resize, as redraw, clipping or picking bounds: Scroll 0x5D1190–0x5D119C (scroll and the listener's centre, SN7); Location the size 0x5FC280, 0x5FC284 and its halves 0x5FC2A0, 0x5FC290 (the view centre for centring 0x4B8940 / 0x4B8B30 and for the screen-to-location rectangle 0x4B9130 when no rectangle is given); Object the view 0x5E2E98–0x5E2EA4, a −256 / +512 margin and two tile spans W / 40 / 2 + 2 and H / 20 / 2 + 2 (0x5E2F50, 0x5E2E60; no reader found outside the module); dialog options the option area 0x5FF520–0x5FF52C; floaters, bubbles, Light, Wall, Iso their redraw rectangles; Target the pick area 0x603BB0 (SU16). Combat-UI places its load indicator (art 601, CH6) at x = window x + width − art width − 2 below the window's top, at init and at every resize. Sector, Tile-Script, Facade, `WP`, TileBlock, Roof, JumpPoint and GFade store only the window handle. None of these decides a game rule. | Original |
| SZ5 | vicinity 0x440FC0 → 0x440FF0 (0x51CEB0 on 0x5E2F24, 0x4B8730, 0x4B8680, 0x4B9130, list 0x440B40); callers 0x44B1F0, 0x44B4E0, 0x45F110, 0x463370, 0x463C60, 0x4A69C0, 0x4A9560, 0x4B3D90, 0x4BD150, 0x4C3770, 0x4F28A0 | Each call reads the game window's rectangle anew and lists the objects of a rectangle of the window's width and height centred on the given location (the window's centre point mapped to the location, then the window's corners). The size is the game window's, not the view's: 800 × 400 in the default launch, 800 × 600 for the whole run under `-fullscreen` (with the bars shown too). Callers: FL3, TG4, NT12, NT10, BT10, OL29, HS17, RT2, TT12, NT11, SU15. This makes PG13's "never depends on a resolution" true for the default launch only. | Original |
| SZ6 | generator check 0x4BA790, placement 0x4BA910 (object rectangle 0x43DFD0, view 0x5FC358–0x5FC364 from 0x4BA380 / 0x4BA430) | A generator whose flag 0x4 is set spawns nothing while its own screen rectangle (0x43DFD0, taken as the object's drawn rectangle; not read in detail) meets the view rectangle; its spawn spots are random spots whose new rectangle must lie outside the view, up to ten tries, then the generator's own spot. The view is the stored view rectangle (800 × 400 by default; under `-fullscreen` the current 800 × 400 or 800 × 600). | Original (a rule tied to what the player can see) |
| SZ7 | restock 0x463C60 (0x43DFD0, 0x5E8808–0x5E8814 from 0x460E70 / 0x460FC0) | BT10's "container on screen" is the container's screen rectangle (0x43DFD0, not read in detail) meeting the Item module's stored view rectangle, the same view as SZ6; its off-screen branch then searches the vicinity (SZ5). | Original (as BT10) |
| SZ8 | fidget event 0x4300D0 (0x430127..0x430173: 0x4B9130, 0x45A950, 0x4302D0) | The event lists the critters of a constant screen rectangle (0, 0, 800 × 400) of the view converted to locations, picks among them and schedules itself again after 4000 ms. The rectangle never follows the window or view size. | Original |
| SZ9 | map draw 0x402E50 (RL11 (4)) | What the view draws locks and loads its sectors (RL11), so a larger view keeps more sectors resident; residency, not a game rule in itself. | Original (RL11's level) |
| SZ10 | sound init 0x41AA30 (0x41AB33..0x41AB62), setters 0x41C690, 0x41C850 | The positional sound parameters are constants given at init: 400, 0, 300, 0 to 0x41C690 and 150, 800, 150, 400 to 0x41C850; no screen size is read. The listener is the view's centre tile (SN7). | Original |
